\TsigAInn(\mathbb{G})=\mathbb{R}
.

The scaling group of
\mathbb{G}
is described in detail in
[
15
]
. We have
\tau^{\mathbb{G}}_{t}(v)=v
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
while
\tau^{\mathbb{G}}_{t}(n)=q^{-2\mathrm{i}{t}}n
. It follows from the uniqueness of the polar decomposition that
\tau^{\mathbb{G}}_{t}
fixes
|n|
and hence multiplies the phase factor of
n
by
q^{-2\mathrm{i}{t}}
. In the isomorphism (
5.1
) this phase factor is easily seen to be the unitary generator of the center
\Linf(\mathbb{T})\otimes\mathds{1}
of
\Linf(\mathbb{G})\cong\Linf(\mathbb{T})\overset{\rule[-1.0pt]{5.0pt}{0.5pt}}{\otimes}\B(\ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z}))
which implies that the non-trivial scaling automorphisms are not inner nor approximately inner, i.e.

\TtauInn(\mathbb{G})=\TtauAInn(\mathbb{G})=\Ttau(\mathbb{G})=\tfrac{\pi}{\log{q}}\mathbb{Z}.

It is known that
\mathbb{G}
is unimodular and hence
\Mod(\mathbb{G})=\mathbb{R}
.

The values of invariants for the dual quantum group
\widehat{\operatorname{E}_{q}(2)}
can be calculated in a similar fashion. However we can use the many results contained in
[
15
]
to make some shortcuts. Denoting
\widehat{\operatorname{E}_{q}(2)}
by
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
we first of all have
\Ttau(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})=\Ttau(\mathbb{G})=\tfrac{\pi}{\log{q}}\mathbb{Z}
. Next we note that by
[
15
, Lemma 2.7.31]
the modular group for the left Haar measure of
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
coincides with
\tau^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}
(this also follows from a more general fact, see
[
21
, Remarks before Lemma 6.3]
). Hence
\Ttau(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})=\Tsig(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})=\tfrac{\pi}{\log{q}}\mathbb{Z}
.

Furthermore
[
15
, Proposition 2.8.4]
gives a direct description of the modular element of
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
, which immediately implies
\Mod(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})=\tfrac{\pi}{\log{q}}\mathbb{Z}
.

The invariants left to determine are
\TtauInn(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
,
\TtauAInn(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
,
\TsigInn(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
, and
\TsigAInn(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
. For this let us recall the presentation of the
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra
\C_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
proposed in
[
15
]
which is slightly different from the one originally introduced in
[
47
]
. Namely in the GNS representation of the left Haar measure, the
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra
\C_{0}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
is the
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra of operators on
\ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z})\otimes\ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z})\otimes\ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z})
generated by
a,a^{-1}
and
b
, where
a=Q^{\scriptscriptstyle{-1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}}\otimes{Q}\otimes\mathds{1}
and
b=Q^{\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}}\otimes{s}\otimes\mathds{1}
(cf.
[
15
, Proposition 2.7.33]
or
[
2
, Proposition 5.2]
). Furthermore, as shown in
[
15
, Proposition 2.6.50]
, we have

\tau^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{t}(a)=a\quad\text{and}\quad\tau^{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}_{t}(b)=q^{-2\mathrm{i}{t}}b

for all
t
which clearly shows that all these automorphisms are inner, implemented e.g. by
\{a^{-2\mathrm{i}{t}}\}_{t\in\mathbb{R}}
. It follows that

\TtauInn(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})=\TtauAInn(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})=\TsigInn(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})=\TsigAInn(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})=\mathbb{R}.

5.2.
The quantum “
az+b
” group

There are three classes of quantum deformations of the classical “
az+b
” group described in
[
50
,
33
]
. They are defined by the choice of the deformation parameter

(1)

q=\mathrm{e}^{\frac{2\pi\mathrm{i}}{N}}
with
N\in\{6,8,\dotsc\}
,

(2)

q\in\left]0,1\right[
,

(3)

q=\mathrm{e}^{\frac{1}{\rho}}
with
\operatorname{Re}{\rho}<0
,
\operatorname{Im}{\rho}=\tfrac{N}{2\pi}
with
N\in\{\pm 2,\pm 4,\dotsc\}
.

Reserving now the symbol
\mathbb{G}
for the quantum “
az+b
” group we have that the
\mathrm{C}^{*}
-algebra
\C_{0}(\mathbb{G})
is generated in the sense of
[
49
]
by two normal unbounded elements
a
and
b
affiliated with
\C_{0}(\mathbb{G})
such that

ab=q^{2}ba,\qquad ab^{*}=b^{*}a

and
\operatorname{Sp}(a),\operatorname{Sp}(b)\subset\overline{\Gamma_{q}}
, where
\Gamma_{q}
is a multiplicative subgroup of
\mathbb{C}\setminus\{0\}
generated by
q
and
\bigl\{q^{\mathrm{i}{t}}\,\bigr|\bigl.t\in\mathbb{R}\bigr\}
(for details see
[
50
,
33
]
). Note for future reference that in case (
2
), we have

\Gamma_{q}=\bigl\{z\in\mathbb{C}\,\bigr|\bigl.\,|z|\in{q{Z}}\bigr\}.

The comultiplication on
\C_{0}(\mathbb{G})
is determined by its values on the two generators:

\Delta(a)=a\otimes{a},\quad\Delta(b)=a\otimes{b}\;\dot{+}\;b\otimes\mathds{1},

where, as in Section
5.1
, the symbol
\dot{+}
indicates the closure of the sum of unbounded elements. The Haar measure of
\mathbb{G}
was determined in
[
42
]
in case (
1
) and in
[
51
]
in all three cases.

It is known that in all cases the locally compact quantum group
\mathbb{G}
is anti-isomorphic to its dual
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
(i.e. isomorphic to
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{\text{\tiny{\rm{op}}}}
). It follows that

T^{\tau}_{\bullet\!}(\mathbb{G})=T^{\tau}_{\bullet\!}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})

for all choices of
\bullet
. Moreover, it was shown in
[
21
, Section 7]
that
\Linf(\mathbb{G})
(and hence also
\Linf(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
) is a factor of type
\mathrm{I}_{\infty}
, so

\TsigInn(\mathbb{G})=\TsigAInn(\mathbb{G})=\TsigInn(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})=\TsigAInn(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})=\mathbb{R}.

It is known that the scaling automorphisms of
\mathbb{G}
are all inner and implemented by
\{|a|^{\mathrm{i}{t}}\}_{t\in\mathbb{R}}
(this is explicitly proved in
[
33
]
for case (
3
) and in
[
51
]
for cases (
1
) and (
2
)). Hence

\TtauInn(\mathbb{G})=\TtauAInn(\mathbb{G})=\TtauInn(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})=\TtauAInn(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})=\mathbb{R},

which by
[
32
, Corollary 2.9.32]
also follows from the fact that
\Linf(\mathbb{G})
is a type
\mathrm{I}_{\infty}
factor. Furthermore, we have
\tau_{t}{G}(a)=a
and
\tau_{t}{G}(b)=q^{2\mathrm{i}{t}}b
for all
t
which immediately implies that
\Ttau(\mathbb{G})=\{0\}
in cases (
1
) and (
3
), while

\Ttau(\mathbb{G})=\tfrac{\pi}{\log{q}}\mathbb{Z}

in case (
2
). Thus, by (
2.2a
) and (
2.2d
) in Proposition
2.3
, we obtain

\Tsig(\mathbb{G})=\{0\}\quad\text{and}\quad\Mod(\mathbb{G})=\{0\}

in cases (
1
) and (
3
). The same holds for
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
(cf. Remark
2.2
(
2
)).

The remaining invariants are
\Tsig(\mathbb{G})
and
\Mod(\mathbb{G})
in case (
2
) (which are the same for
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
). For the first one we note that by the discussion preceding
[
21
, Proposition 7.7]
we have
\sigma^{\psi}_{t}(x)=|b|^{2\mathrm{i}{t}}x|b|^{-2\mathrm{i}{t}}
for all
x\in\Linf(\mathbb{G})
and it follows that
\Tsig(\mathbb{G})=\{0\}
in cases (
1
) and (
3
), while
\Tsig(\mathbb{G})=\tfrac{\pi}{\log{q}}\mathbb{Z}
in case (
2
).

Now that we know that
\Tsig(\mathbb{G})=\Ttau(\mathbb{G})
(and that
\mathbb{G}
is anti-self-dual), equation (
2.2a
) implies that
\Tsig(\mathbb{G})\subset\Mod(\mathbb{G})
, while (
2.2d
) gives
\Mod(\mathbb{G})\subset\tfrac{1}{2}\Tsig(\mathbb{G})
. In other words

\tfrac{\pi}{\log{q}}\mathbb{Z}\subset\Mod(\mathbb{G})\subset\tfrac{\pi}{2\log{q}}\mathbb{Z}

(and consequently
\Mod(\mathbb{G})
is either of the two groups above). To see that it is
\tfrac{\pi}{\log{q}}\mathbb{Z}
let us take advantage of the many results in
[
21
]
.

More precisely by
[
21
, Proposition 5.5]
and the explicit determination of the Plancherel objects for
\mathbb{G}
in
[
21
, Section 7]
we have that
t\in\Mod(\mathbb{G})
if and only if

(5.2)

|qa^{-1}b|^{-2\mathrm{i}{t}}|b|^{2\mathrm{i}{t}}=\mathds{1}

(for this we also need the fact that the scaling constant is trivial in case (
2
), cf.
[
51
,
21
]
). In order to determine for which
t
(
5.2
) holds we use the fact that the GNS representation of
\Linf(\mathbb{G})
is equivalent to the representation on
\Ltwo(\Gamma_{q})
with (the images of)
a
and
b
determined by

(b\psi)(\gamma)=\gamma\psi(\gamma),\qquad\psi\in\Ltwo(\Gamma_{q}),\>\gamma\in\Gamma_{q}

and denoting the polar decomposition of
a
by
(\operatorname{Phase}a)|a|
we have

\left.\begin{array}[]{r@{\:=\:}l}\bigl((\operatorname{Phase}a)\psi\bigr)(\gamma)\>=\>&\psi(q\gamma)\\[3.0pt]
\bigl(|a|^{\mathrm{i}{t}}\psi\bigr)(\gamma)\>=\>&\psi(q^{\mathrm{i}{t}}\gamma)\end{array}\right.,\qquad\psi\in\Ltwo(\Gamma_{q}),\>\gamma\in\Gamma_{q},\>t\in\mathbb{R}.

Now let
\{g_{k,l}\}_{k,l\in\mathbb{Z}}
be the orthonormal basis of
\Ltwo(\Gamma_{q})
defined by

g_{k,l}(\gamma)=\begin{cases}\left(\frac{\gamma}{|\gamma|}\right)^{k}&|\gamma|=q^{l}\\
0&|\gamma|\neq{q^{l}}\end{cases}.

Then one can check by direct computation that
|qa^{-1}b|g_{k,l}=q^{l-k}g_{k,l}
and
|b|g_{k,l}=q^{l}g_{k,l}
for all
k,l
and consequently

|qa^{-1}b|^{-2\mathrm{i}{t}}|b|^{2\mathrm{i}{t}}g_{k,l}=q^{-2\mathrm{i}{t}(l-k)+2\mathrm{i}{t}l}g_{k,l}=q^{2\mathrm{i}{t}k}g_{k,l},\qquad{k,l}\in\mathbb{Z},

so that (
5.2
) holds if and only if
t\in\tfrac{\pi}{\log{q}}\mathbb{Z}
.

5.3.
The quantum group
\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}

Let
F\in\operatorname{GL}(N,\mathbb{C})
(
N\geq{2}
) be an arbitrary invertible matrix and
\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}
the corresponding free unitary quantum group whose Haar measure
{\boldsymbol{h}}_{\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}}
and scaling automorphisms
\tau^{\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}}_{t}
will throughout this section be denoted by
{\boldsymbol{h}}
and
\tau_{t}
. We will also see
\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
as a subspace of
\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
via the GNS map of
{\boldsymbol{h}}
. As in
[
3
]
(and Section
3.1
) we identify
\Irr{\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}}
with the free product monoid
\mathbb{Z}_{+}\star\mathbb{Z}_{+}
with generators
\alpha
and
\beta
corresponding to the defining representation and its conjugate respectively.

We want to calculate
T^{\tau}_{\bullet}
invariants of
\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}
. First, we establish an analogue of
[
10
, Proposition 32]
for the scaling group. We follow closely
[
10
, Appendix]
.

As in Section
3.1
, for
x\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}\star\mathbb{Z}_{+}
choose a representative
u^{x}
which acts on
\mathscr{H}_{x}
and define a state
\omega_{x}
on
\B(\mathscr{H}_{x})
by

\omega_{x}(T)=\tfrac{1}{\qdim{x}}\Tr(T\uprho_{x}^{-1}),\qquad{T}\in\B(\mathscr{H}_{x})

which satisfies
[
28
, Proof of Theorem 1.4.3]

({\boldsymbol{h}}\otimes\mathrm{id})\bigl({u^{x}}^{*}(\mathds{1}\otimes{T})u^{x}\bigr)=\omega_{x}(T)\mathds{1},\qquad{T}\in\B(\mathscr{H}_{x}).

Next, let
\mathscr{K}_{x}
be the GNS Hilbert space defined by
\omega_{x}
, i.e.
\mathscr{K}_{x}
is equal to
\B(\mathscr{H}_{x})
as a vector space, with inner product
{\left\langle{T}\,\vline\,T^{\prime}\right\rangle}=\omega_{x}(T^{*}T^{\prime})
(
T,T^{\prime}\in\mathscr{K}_{x}
). Since

(5.3)

(\sigma^{{\boldsymbol{h}}}_{t}\otimes\mathrm{id})u^{x}=(\mathds{1}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{\mathrm{i}{t}})u^{x}(\mathds{1}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{\mathrm{i}{t}}),\qquad{t}\in\mathbb{R},\>x\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}\star\mathbb{Z}_{+}

(
[
28
, Page 30]
), we obtain

(5.4)

({\boldsymbol{h}}\otimes\omega_{x})\bigl(u^{x}(a\otimes\mathds{1}){u^{x}}^{*}\bigr)={\boldsymbol{h}}(a),\qquad{a}\in\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}).

Indeed,

\displaystyle({\boldsymbol{h}}\otimes\omega_{x})\bigl(u^{x}(a\otimes\mathds{1}){u^{x}}^{*}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\Bigl({\boldsymbol{h}}\otimes\tfrac{\Tr}{\qdim{x}}\Bigr)\bigl(u^{x}(a\otimes\mathds{1}){u^{x}}^{*}(\mathds{1}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{-1})\bigr)

\displaystyle=\Bigr({\boldsymbol{h}}\otimes\tfrac{\Tr}{\qdim{x}}\Bigr)\bigl((a\otimes\mathds{1}){u^{x}}^{*}(\mathds{1}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{-1})(\sigma^{{\boldsymbol{h}}}_{-\mathrm{i}}\otimes\mathrm{id})(u^{x})\bigr)

\displaystyle=\Bigl({\boldsymbol{h}}\otimes\tfrac{\Tr}{\qdim{x}}\Bigr)\bigl((a\otimes\mathds{1}){u^{x}}^{*}u^{x}(\mathds{1}\otimes\uprho_{x})\bigr)={\boldsymbol{h}}(a).

For
x\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}\star\mathbb{Z}_{+}
and
t\in\mathbb{R}
define
V_{x}^{\tau,t}\colon\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})\to\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})\otimes\mathscr{K}_{x}
as the isometric extension of

\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})\ni{a}\longmapsto(\tau_{t}\otimes\mathrm{id})(u^{x})(a\otimes\mathds{1}){u^{x}}^{*}.

To see that this map is isometric take
a,b\in\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})\subset\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
. Then using (
5.4
) we obtain

\displaystyle{\left\langle{V^{\tau,t}_{x}(a)}\,\vline\,V^{\tau,t}_{x}(b)\right\rangle}

\displaystyle=({\boldsymbol{h}}\otimes\omega_{x})\bigl(u^{x}(a^{*}\otimes\mathds{1})(\tau_{t}\otimes\mathrm{id})({u^{x}}^{*}u^{x})(b\otimes\mathds{1}){u^{x}}^{*}\bigr)

\displaystyle=({\boldsymbol{h}}\otimes\omega_{x})\bigl(u^{x}(a^{*}b\otimes\mathds{1}){u^{x}}^{*}\bigr)={\boldsymbol{h}}(a^{*}b)={\left\langle{a}\,\vline\,b\right\rangle}.

The following proposition is (together with its proof) an analogue of
[
10
, Proposition 32]
with the modular group replaced by the scaling group.

Proposition 5.1
.

For
a\in\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
and
t\in\mathbb{R}
we have

\bigl\|a-{\boldsymbol{h}}(a)\mathds{1}\bigr\|_{2}\leq{14}\max\Bigl\{\bigl\|a\otimes\mathds{1}-V^{\tau,t}_{\alpha\beta}(a)\bigr\|,\bigl\|a\otimes\mathds{1}-V^{\tau,t}_{\alpha^{2}\beta}(a)\bigr\|,\bigl\|a\otimes\mathds{1}-V^{\tau,t}_{\beta\alpha}(a)\bigr\|\Bigr\}.

Before continuing let us note that the operator
\uprho_{\alpha}
can be expressed in terms of
F
(with an appropriate identification of
\mathscr{H}_{\beta}
with
\mathbb{C}^{N}
on which
F
acts). Indeed, if
\Tr(F^{*}F)=\Tr\bigl((F^{*}F)^{-1}\bigr)
we have

\uprho_{\alpha}=(F^{*}F)^{\top}

(
[
28
, Example 1.4.2]
)
while in the general case

\uprho_{\alpha}=\lambda(F^{*}F)^{\top},

where

\lambda=\sqrt{\tfrac{\Tr((F^{*}F)^{-1})}{\Tr(F^{*}F)}}.

For each
x\in\Irr{\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}}
choose a basis of the Hilbert space
\mathscr{H}_{x}
which diagonalizes
\uprho_{x}
and let the corresponding rank one operators be denoted by
e^{x}_{i,j}\in\B(\mathscr{H}_{x})
.

Theorem 5.2
.

Assume
\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}
is not of Kac type. We have

(5.5)

\TtauAInn(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})=\TtauInn(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})=\Ttau(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})=\bigcap_{\Lambda\in\operatorname{Sp}(F^{*}F\otimes(F^{*}F)^{-1})\setminus\{1\}}\,\tfrac{2\pi}{\log{\Lambda}}\mathbb{Z}.

Note that the equality
\TtauInn(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})=\Ttau(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
shows in particular that Conjecture
1
holds for
\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}
and all invertible matrices
F
.

Proof of Theorem
5.2
.

Take
t\in\TtauAInn(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
and let
(v_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of unitaries in
\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
such that
\Ad(v_{n})\xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{}\tau_{t}
. This means that for any
\omega\in\Lone(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
we have

(5.6)

\bigl\|\omega\circ\Ad(v_{n})-\omega\circ\tau_{t}\bigr\|\xrightarrow[n\to\infty]{}0.

Assume by contradiction that
t\not\in\Ttau(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
. Then there exists
k,l\in\{1,\dotsc,\dim{\alpha}\}
with

\tau_{t}(u^{\alpha}_{k,l})=\uprho_{\alpha,k}^{\mathrm{i}{t}}\uprho_{\alpha,l}^{-\mathrm{i}{t}}u^{\alpha}_{k,l}

and
\uprho_{\alpha,k}^{\mathrm{i}{t}}\uprho_{\alpha,l}^{-\mathrm{i}{t}}\neq{1}
.

Set
c=|1-\uprho_{\alpha,k}^{\mathrm{i}{t}}\uprho_{\alpha,l}^{-\mathrm{i}{t}}|>0
and define
\varepsilon>0
via

\varepsilon=\tfrac{1}{4}\min\biggl\{1,c^{2}\tfrac{\uprho_{\alpha,l}^{2}}{(\qdim{\alpha})^{2}}\Bigl(1+(3+\|\uprho_{\alpha}\|^{2})28\max\bigl\{(\dim{\alpha\beta})^{2},(\dim{\alpha^{2}\beta})^{2},(\dim{\beta\alpha})^{2}\bigr\}\Bigr)^{-2}\biggr\}.

Next, from (
5.6
) we obtain
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that

(5.7)

\bigl\|{\boldsymbol{h}}\bigl(\cdot\,{u^{x}_{i,j}}^{*})\circ\Ad(v_{n})-{\boldsymbol{h}}(\cdot\,{u^{x}_{i,j}}^{*})\circ\tau_{t}\bigr\|\leq\varepsilon

for
x\in\{\alpha\beta,\alpha^{2}\beta,\beta\alpha\}
and
i,j\in\{1,\dotsc,\dim{x}\}
. Using Proposition
5.1
and the fact that
V^{\tau,t}_{x}
is isometric we have

\displaystyle\bigl\|v_{n}^{*}-{\boldsymbol{h}}(v_{n}^{*})\mathds{1}\bigr\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 14^{2}\max_{x\in\{\alpha\beta,\alpha^{2}\beta,\beta\alpha\}}\bigl\|v_{n}^{*}\otimes\mathds{1}-V^{\tau,-t}_{x}(v_{n}^{*})\bigr\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle=14^{2}\max_{x\in\{\alpha\beta,\alpha^{2}\beta,\beta\alpha\}}\bigl(2-2\operatorname{Re}{\left\langle{v_{n}^{*}\otimes\mathds{1}}\,\vline\,V_{x}^{\tau,-t}(v_{n}^{*})\right\rangle}\bigr)

\displaystyle=14^{2}\max_{x\in\{\alpha\beta,\alpha^{2}\beta,\beta\alpha\}}\Bigl(2-2\operatorname{Re}({\boldsymbol{h}}\otimes\omega_{x})\bigl((v_{n}\otimes\mathds{1})(\tau_{-t}\otimes\mathrm{id})(u^{x})(v_{n}^{*}\otimes\mathds{1}){u^{x}}^{*}\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle=14^{2}\max_{x\in\{\alpha\beta,\alpha^{2}\beta,\beta\alpha\}}\biggl(2-2\sum_{i,j,r,s=1}^{\dim{x}}\operatorname{Re}\Bigl({\boldsymbol{h}}\bigl(\Ad(v_{n})\bigl(\tau_{-t}(u^{x}_{i,j})\bigr){u^{x}_{r,s}}^{*}\bigr)\omega_{x}(e^{x}_{i,j}e^{x}_{s,r})\Bigr)\biggr)

\displaystyle=14^{2}\max_{x\in\{\alpha\beta,\alpha^{2}\beta,\beta\alpha\}}\biggl(2-2\sum_{i,j,r,s=1}^{\dim{x}}\operatorname{Re}\Bigl({\boldsymbol{h}}\bigl(\tau_{t}(\tau_{-t}(u^{x}_{i,j})){u^{x}_{r,s}}^{*}\bigr)\,\omega_{x}(e^{x}_{i,j}e^{x}_{s,r})\Bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad-2\sum_{i,j,r,s=1}^{\dim{x}}\operatorname{Re}\Bigl({\boldsymbol{h}}\bigl(\Ad(v_{n})(\tau_{-t}(u^{x}_{i,j})){u^{x}_{r,s}}^{*}\bigr)-{\boldsymbol{h}}\bigl(\tau_{t}(\tau_{-t}(u^{x}_{i,j})){u^{x}_{r,s}}^{*}\bigr)\omega_{x}(e^{x}_{i,j}e^{x}_{s,r})\Bigr)\biggr).

The first term is

\displaystyle 2-2\sum_{i,j,r,s=1}^{\dim{x}}\operatorname{Re}\bigl({\boldsymbol{h}}(u^{x}_{i,j}{u^{x}_{r,s}}^{*})\omega_{x}(e^{x}_{i,j}e^{x}_{s,r})\bigr)=2-2\operatorname{Re}({\boldsymbol{h}}\otimes\omega_{x})(u^{x}{u^{x}}^{*})=0

for each
x
, while the second can be bounded by

\displaystyle 2\cdot 14^{2}

\displaystyle\max_{x\in\{\alpha\beta,\alpha^{2}\beta,\beta\alpha\}}\sum_{i,j,r,s=1}^{\dim{x}}\Bigl|{\boldsymbol{h}}\Bigl(\Ad(v_{n})\bigl(\tau_{-t}(u^{x}_{i,j})\bigr){u^{x}_{r,s}}^{*}\Bigr)-{\boldsymbol{h}}\Bigl(\tau_{t}\bigl(\tau_{-t}(u^{x}_{i,j})\bigr){u^{x}_{r,s}}^{*}\Bigr)\omega_{x}(e^{x}_{i,j}e^{x}_{s,r})\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq 2\cdot 14^{2}\max_{x\in\{\alpha\beta,\alpha^{2}\beta,\beta\alpha\}}\sum_{i,j,r,s=1}^{\dim{x}}\bigl\|{\boldsymbol{h}}(\cdot\,{u^{x}_{r,s}}^{*})\circ\Ad(v_{n})-{\boldsymbol{h}}(\cdot\,{u^{x}_{r,s}}^{*})\circ\tau_{t}\bigr\|\bigl\|\tau_{-t}(u^{x}_{i,j})\bigr\|\bigl|\omega_{x}(e^{x}_{i,j}e^{x}_{s,r})\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq 2\cdot 14^{2}\biggl(\max_{x\in\{\alpha\beta,\alpha^{2}\beta,\beta\alpha\}}(\dim{x})^{4}\biggr)\varepsilon,

and consequently

(5.8)

\displaystyle\bigl\|v_{n}^{*}-{\boldsymbol{h}}(v_{n}^{*})\mathds{1}\bigr\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\cdot 14\biggl(\max_{x\in\{\alpha\beta,\alpha^{2}\beta,\beta\alpha\}}(\dim{x})^{2}\biggr)\sqrt{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\leq 28\biggl(\max_{x\in\{\alpha\beta,\alpha^{2}\beta,\beta\alpha\}}(\dim{x})^{2}\biggr)\sqrt{\varepsilon}.

It follows that

(5.9)

1=\|v_{n}^{*}\|_{2}\leq\bigl\|v_{n}^{*}-{\boldsymbol{h}}(v_{n}^{*})\mathds{1}\bigr\|_{2}+\bigl|{\boldsymbol{h}}(v_{n}^{*})\bigr|\leq 28\biggl(\max_{x\in\{\alpha\beta,\alpha^{2}\beta,\beta\alpha\}}(\dim{x})^{2}\biggr)\sqrt{\varepsilon}+\bigl|{\boldsymbol{h}}(v_{n}^{*})\bigr|.

Observe that

\displaystyle\bigl|{\boldsymbol{h}}(v_{n}u^{\alpha}_{k,l}

\displaystyle v_{n}^{*}{u^{\alpha}_{k,l}}^{*})-{\boldsymbol{h}}(u^{\alpha}_{k,l}{u^{\alpha}_{k,l}}^{*})\bigr|\leq\Bigl|{\boldsymbol{h}}\Bigl(\bigl(v_{n}-{\boldsymbol{h}}(v_{n})\mathds{1}\bigr)u^{\alpha}_{k,l}v_{n}^{*}{u^{\alpha}_{k,l}}^{*}\Bigr)\Bigl|+\Bigl|{\boldsymbol{h}}\Bigl(u^{\alpha}_{k,l}\bigl({\boldsymbol{h}}(v_{n})v_{n}^{*}-\mathds{1}\bigr){u^{\alpha}_{k,l}}^{*}\Bigr)\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|v_{n}^{*}-{\boldsymbol{h}}(v_{n}^{*})\mathds{1}\bigr\|_{2}+\Bigl|{\boldsymbol{h}}\Bigl(u^{\alpha}_{k,l}\bigl({\boldsymbol{h}}(v_{n})v_{n}^{*}-|{\boldsymbol{h}}(v_{n}^{*})|^{2}\mathds{1}\bigr){u^{\alpha}_{k,l}}^{*}\Bigr)\Bigr|+\Bigl|\bigl|{\boldsymbol{h}}(v_{n}^{*})\bigr|^{2}-1\Bigr|\bigl|{\boldsymbol{h}}(u^{\alpha}_{k,l}{u^{\alpha}_{k,l}}^{*})\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|v_{n}^{*}-{\boldsymbol{h}}(v_{n}^{*})\mathds{1}\bigr\|_{2}+\bigl|{\boldsymbol{h}}(v_{n})\bigr|\Bigl|{\boldsymbol{h}}\Bigl(u^{\alpha}_{k,l}\bigl(v_{n}^{*}-{\boldsymbol{h}}(v_{n}^{*})\mathds{1}\bigr){u^{\alpha}_{k,l}}^{*}\Bigr)\Bigr|+\Bigl|\bigl|{\boldsymbol{h}}(v_{n}^{*})\bigr|^{2}-1\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\Bigl(1+\bigl\|\sigma^{{\boldsymbol{h}}}_{-\mathrm{i}}(u^{\alpha}_{k,l})\bigr\|\Bigr)\bigl\|v_{n}^{*}-{\boldsymbol{h}}(v_{n}^{*})\mathds{1}\bigr\|_{2}+\Bigl|\bigl|{\boldsymbol{h}}(v_{n}^{*})\bigr|^{2}-1\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\bigl(1+\|\uprho_{\alpha}\|^{2}\bigr)\bigl\|v_{n}^{*}-{\boldsymbol{h}}(v_{n}^{*})\mathds{1}\bigr\|_{2}+2\Bigl|\bigl|{\boldsymbol{h}}(v_{n}^{*})\bigr|-1\Bigr|,

and using (
5.8
), (
5.9
)

(5.10)

\bigl|{\boldsymbol{h}}(v_{n}u^{\alpha}_{k,l}v_{n}^{*}{u^{\alpha}_{k,l}}^{*})-{\boldsymbol{h}}(u^{\alpha}_{k,l}{u^{\alpha}_{k,l}}^{*})\bigr|\leq\bigl(3+\|\uprho_{\alpha}\|^{2}\bigr)28\biggl(\max_{x\in\{\alpha\beta,\alpha^{2}\beta,\beta\alpha\}}(\dim{x})^{2}\biggr)\sqrt{\varepsilon}.

Finally, using (
5.7
) and (
5.10
), we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon

\displaystyle\geq\Bigl|{\boldsymbol{h}}(\cdot\,{u^{\alpha}_{k,l}}^{*})\circ\bigl(\Ad(v_{n})-\tau_{t}\bigr)(u^{\alpha}_{k,l})\Bigr|=\Bigl|{\boldsymbol{h}}(v_{n}u^{\alpha}_{k,l}v_{n}^{*}{u^{\alpha}_{k,l}}^{*})-{\boldsymbol{h}}\bigl(\tau_{t}(u^{\alpha}_{k,l}){u^{\alpha}_{k,l}}^{*}\bigr)\Bigr|

\displaystyle\geq\bigl|{\boldsymbol{h}}(u^{\alpha}_{k,l}{u^{\alpha}_{k,l}}^{*})-\uprho_{\alpha,k}^{\mathrm{i}{t}}\uprho_{\alpha,l}^{-\mathrm{i}{t}}{\boldsymbol{h}}(u^{\alpha}_{k,l}{u^{\alpha}_{k,l}}^{*})\bigr|-\bigl|{\boldsymbol{h}}(v_{n}u^{\alpha}_{k,l}v_{n}^{*}{u^{\alpha}_{k,l}}^{*})-{\boldsymbol{h}}(u^{\alpha}_{k,l}{u^{\alpha}_{k,l}}^{*})\bigr|

\displaystyle\geq{c}\,\tfrac{\uprho_{\alpha,l}}{\qdim{\alpha}}-\bigl(3+\|\uprho_{\alpha}\|^{2}\bigr)28\biggl(\max_{x\in\{\alpha\beta,\alpha^{2}\beta,\beta\alpha\}}(\dim{x})^{2}\biggr)\sqrt{\varepsilon},

and using definition of
\varepsilon
we arrive at

\displaystyle c

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{\qdim{\alpha}}{\uprho_{\alpha,l}}\Bigl(\varepsilon+\bigl(3+\|\uprho_{\alpha}\|^{2}\bigr)28\bigl(\,\max_{x\in\{\alpha\beta,\alpha^{2}\beta,\beta\alpha\}}(\dim{x})^{2}\bigr)\sqrt{\varepsilon}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{\qdim{\alpha}}{\uprho_{\alpha,l}}\Bigl(1+\bigl(3+\|\uprho_{\alpha}\|^{2}\bigr)28\bigl(\,\max_{x\in\{\alpha\beta,\alpha^{2}\beta,\beta\alpha\}}(\dim{x})^{2}\bigr)\Bigr)\sqrt{\varepsilon}\leq\tfrac{c}{2}.

Thus we can conclude that
t\in\Ttau(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
.

It is now left to identify this group. Since matrix elements of
u^{\alpha}
generate
\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
, a number
t\in\mathbb{R}
belongs to
\Ttau(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
if and only if
u^{\alpha}_{i,j}=\tau_{t}(u^{\alpha}_{i,j})=\bigl(\tfrac{\uprho_{\alpha,i}}{\uprho_{\alpha,j}}\bigr)^{\mathrm{i}{t}}u^{\alpha}_{i,j}
for all
i,j\in\{1,\dotsc,N\}
, i.e. if and only if
\bigl(\tfrac{\uprho_{\alpha,i}}{\uprho_{\alpha,j}}\bigr)^{\mathrm{i}{t}}=1
for all
i,j\in\{1,\dotsc,N\}
and this condition is equivalent to

\forall\>\Lambda\in\operatorname{Sp}(\uprho_{\alpha}\otimes\uprho_{\alpha}^{-1})\setminus\{1\}\quad{t}\in\tfrac{2\pi}{\log{\Lambda}}\mathbb{Z}.

(Note that if
\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}
is of Kac type then this condition is vacuous and
\Ttau(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})=\mathbb{R}
.) Finally recall that
\uprho_{\alpha}=\sqrt{\tfrac{\Tr((F^{*}F)^{-1})}{\Tr(F^{*}F)}}(F^{*}F)^{\top}
. The claim (
5.5
) follows from

\operatorname{Sp}(\uprho_{\alpha}\otimes\uprho_{\alpha}^{-1})=\operatorname{Sp}\Bigl((F^{*}F)^{\top}\otimes\bigl((F^{*}F)^{\top}\bigr)^{-1}\Bigr)=\operatorname{Sp}\bigl(F^{*}F\otimes(F^{*}F)^{-1}\bigr).\qed

Remark 5.3
.

Equality
\TtauInn(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})=\TtauAInn(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
follows also from the fact that
\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
is full (
[
10
, Theorem 33]
).

Clearly
\Mod(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})=\mathbb{R}
, but
\widehat{\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}}
is not always unimodular. In order to determine
\Mod(\widehat{\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}})
let us recall that the modular element of a discrete quantum group was already described in
[
30
, Theorem 3.3 and Remark on page 402]
(cf. the proof of Theorem
4.12
in Section
4.2
). Namely, if
\mathbbl{\Gamma}
is any discrete quantum group with

\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbl{\Gamma})=\prod_{x\in\Irr{\widehat{\mathbbl{\Gamma}}}}\B(\mathscr{H}^{x})

then the modular element of
\mathbbl{\Gamma}
is the element affiliated with
\ell^{\infty}(\mathbbl{\Gamma})
defined as
\bigoplus\limits_{x\in\Irr{\widehat{\mathbbl{\Gamma}}}}\uprho_{x}^{2}
. It follows that
\Mod(\mathbbl{\Gamma})
is the set of
t\in\mathbb{R}
such that
\uprho_{x}^{2\mathrm{i}{t}}=\mathds{1}
for all
x\in\Irr{\widehat{\mathbbl{\Gamma}}}
. Clearly this is the same as the set of those
t\in\mathbb{R}
for which
\uprho_{V}^{2\mathrm{i}{t}}=\mathds{1}
for all finite dimensional representations
V
of
\widehat{\mathbbl{\Gamma}}
. If
\widehat{\mathbbl{\Gamma}}
happens to be a matrix quantum group, i.e. all representations are subrepresentations of tensor products of the fundamental representation
U
and its conjugate
\overline{U}
, we find that
\Mod(\mathbbl{\Gamma})
is the set of those
t\in\mathbb{R}
which satisfy
\uprho_{V}^{\mathrm{i}{t}}=\mathds{1}
for all
V
which are finite tensor products of
U
and
\overline{U}
.

Returning to
\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}
, let
V
be a finite tensor product of
u^{\alpha}
and
u^{\beta}
. Then
\uprho_{V}
is the corresponding tensor product of
\uprho_{\alpha}
and
\uprho_{\beta}=\uprho_{\overline{\alpha}}
(the latter being the transpose of
\uprho_{\alpha}^{-1}
, cf.
[
28
, Proposition 1.4.7]
). Thus
\uprho_{V}^{2\mathrm{i}{t}}=\mathds{1}
if and only if
\uprho_{\alpha}^{2\mathrm{i}{t}}=\mathds{1}
.

Thus, if
\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}
is not of Kac type then

(5.11)

\Mod(\widehat{\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}})=\bigcap_{\Lambda\in\operatorname{Sp}(F^{*}F)\setminus\{\lambda^{-1}\}}\tfrac{\pi}{\log{\lambda}+\log{\Lambda}}\mathbb{Z}

where
\lambda=\sqrt{\tfrac{\Tr((F^{*}F)^{-1})}{\Tr(F^{*}F)}}
. Obviously if
\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}
is of Kac type then
\Mod(\widehat{\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}})=\mathbb{R}
.

In view of Remark
2.5
and the fact that
\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
is full, which means that
\operatorname{Inn}(\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}))=\operatorname{\overline{Inn}}(\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}))
, the one remaining invariant from our list is
\TsigInn(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})=T(\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}))
.

In order to compute this invariant, we recall again the main result of
[
10
, Appendix]
which says that for any
F\in\operatorname{GL}(N,\mathbb{C})
the von Neumann algebra is
\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
is a full factor and its invariant
\Sd
(
[
7
, Definition 1.2]
) is the multiplicative subgroup of
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
generated by the eigenvalues of
\uprho_{\alpha}\otimes\uprho_{\alpha}
.

Now the fact that
\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
is full lets us conclude by
[
7
, Corollary 4.11]
that
S(\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}))
is the closure of
\Sd(\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}))
. In particular it cannot be the set
\{0,1\}
and it follows that
\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
is not a factor of type
\mathrm{III}_{0}
. Furthermore it is not a factor of type
\mathrm{II}_{\infty}
because then
S(\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}))
would be
\{1\}
, but this would mean that
\uprho_{\alpha}=\mathds{1}
and
\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}
is then of Kac type, which makes
\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
a type
\mathrm{II}_{1}
factor.

Consequently
\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
is either a factor of type
\mathrm{II}_{1}
or of type
\mathrm{III}_{\mu}
with
\mu\in\left]0,1\right]
. The first case happens when
\uprho_{\alpha}=\mathds{1}
, then
T(\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}))=\mathbb{R}
. In all the latter cases the Connes invariant
T(\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}))
is determined by the invariant
S(\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}))
(
[
8
, Corollary 3.12, Proposition 3.7]
). More precisely, in case the subgroup of
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
generated by the eigenvalues of
\uprho_{\alpha}\otimes\uprho_{\alpha}
is dense (case
\mathrm{III}_{1}
) we have
T(\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}))=\{0\}
. On the other hand, if this subgroup is non-trivial, but not dense, then it is of the form
\{\mu^{n}\,|\,n\in\mathbb{Z}\}
for some
\mu\in\left]0,1\right[
and
\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
is a type
\mathrm{III}_{\mu}
factor with Connes’ invariant
S(\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}))=\{\mu^{n}\,|\,n\in\mathbb{Z}\}
. Consequently
T(\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}))=\tfrac{2\pi}{\log{\mu}}\mathbb{Z}
.

We have calculated all the modular invariants of
\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}
and its dual in terms of eigenvalues of
F^{*}F
. We finish this section with two results: the first proposition tells that
\Mod(\widehat{\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}})
is almost determined by the type of
\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
, next we describe some interesting examples.

Proposition 5.4
.

(1)

If
\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
is a factor of type
\mathrm{II}_{1}
then
\operatorname{Mod}(\widehat{\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}})=\mathbb{R}
.

(2)

If
\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
is a factor of type
\mathrm{III}_{\mu}
for some
\mu\in\left]0,1\right[
, then either
\Mod(\widehat{\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}})=\tfrac{\pi}{\log{\mu}}\mathbb{Z}
or
\Mod(\widehat{\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}})=\tfrac{2\pi}{\log{\mu}}\mathbb{Z}
.

(3)

If
\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
is a factor of type
\mathrm{III}_{1}
, then
\Mod(\widehat{\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}})=\{0\}
.

Proof.

The first case is trivial, as then
\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}
is of Kac type. Assume that
\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
is a factor of type
\mathrm{III}_{\mu}
for some
\mu\in\left]0,1\right[
. According to the discussion above, this means that
\operatorname{Sp}(\uprho_{\alpha}\otimes\uprho_{\alpha})\subset\{\mu^{n}\,|\,n\in\mathbb{Z}\}
, so in particular
\operatorname{Sp}(\uprho_{\alpha})=\operatorname{Sp}(\lambda{F^{*}F})=\bigl\{\mu^{k_{1}\>\!\!/2},\dotsc,\mu^{k_{N}\>\!\!/2}\bigr\}
for certain
k_{1},\dotsc,k_{N}\in\mathbb{Z}
and, furthermore, the set
\bigl\{\mu^{(k_{i}+k_{j})\>\!\!/2}\,\bigr|\bigl.\,1\leq{i,j}\leq{N}\bigr\}
generates
\{\mu^{n}\,|\,n\in\mathbb{Z}\}
. Write
g=\operatorname{gcd}\bigl(\{|k_{1}|,\dotsc,|k_{N}|\}\setminus\{0\}\bigr)
. Then using (
5.11
) we have
\Mod(\widehat{\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}})=\bigcap\limits_{i=1,\>k_{i}\neq{0}}^{N}\tfrac{2\pi}{k_{i}\log{\mu}}\mathbb{Z}=\tfrac{2\pi}{g\log{\mu}}\mathbb{Z}
, and hence we need to show that
g\in\{1,2\}
. Write
k_{i}=gk_{i}^{\prime}
(
1\leq{i}\leq{N}
) and
g=2^{m}g^{\prime}
, where
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
and
g^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
is not divisible by
2
. Observe that for
1\leq{i,j}\leq{N}
we have
\tfrac{k_{i}+k_{j}}{2}=2^{m-1}g^{\prime}(k_{i}^{\prime}+k_{j}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}
. Since
g^{\prime}
is not divisible by
2
, it must be the case that
2^{m-1}(k^{\prime}_{i}+k^{\prime}_{j})\in\mathbb{Z}
. If
g^{\prime}\neq{1}
then
\bigl\{\mu^{(k_{i}+k_{j})\>\!\!/2}\,\bigr|\bigl.\,1\leq{i,j}\leq{N}\bigr\}\subset\bigl\{\mu^{g^{\prime}n}\,\bigr|\bigl.\,n\in\mathbb{Z}\bigr\}
, which is a proper subgroup of
\{\mu^{n}\,|\,n\in\mathbb{Z}\}
. This is a contradiction. It follows that
g^{\prime}=1
and
g=2^{m}
. If
m\geq{2}
then similarly
\bigl\{\mu^{(k_{i}+k_{j})\>\!\!/2}\,\bigr|\bigl.\,1\leq{i,j}\leq{N}\bigr\}\subset\bigl\{\mu^{2^{m-1}n}\,\bigr|\bigl.\,n\in\mathbb{Z}\bigr\}
and consequently
g\in\{1,2\}
as claimed.

Assume now that
\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
is a type
\mathrm{III}_{1}
factor, i.e.
\operatorname{Sp}(\uprho_{\alpha}\otimes\uprho_{\alpha})
generates a dense subgroup of
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
. Assume by contradiction that
\Mod(\widehat{\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}})\neq\{0\}
. Then, since
\Mod(\widehat{\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}})
is closed and not equal to
\mathbb{R}
(this would mean that
\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}
is of Kac type), it is a cyclic subgroup of
\mathbb{R}
, say
\Mod(\widehat{\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}})=c\mathbb{Z}
for some
c>0
. Let
\operatorname{Sp}(\uprho_{\alpha})=\operatorname{Sp}(\lambda{F^{*}F})=\{x_{1},\dotsc,x_{N}\}
. It follows from (
5.11
) that for any
1\leq{i}\leq{N}
such that
x_{i}\neq{1}
we have
c=\tfrac{\pi{k_{i}}}{\log{x_{i}}}
for some
k_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}
, or equivalently,
x_{i}=\mathrm{e}^{\pi{k_{i}/c}}
. If
x_{i}=1
then we also have
x_{i}=\mathrm{e}^{\pi{k_{i}/c}}
with
k_{i}=0
. Consequently for any
1\leq{i,j}\leq{N}

x_{i}x_{j}=\mathrm{e}^{\pi(k_{i}+k_{j})/c}\in\bigl\{\mathrm{e}^{\pi{n/c}}\,\bigr|\bigl.\,n\in\mathbb{Z}\bigr\}

and
\operatorname{Sp}(\uprho_{\alpha}\otimes\uprho_{\alpha})
does not generate a dense subgroup. This contradiction shows that we must have
\Mod(\widehat{\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}})=\{0\}
.
∎

We finish this section with examples which in particular show that all the possibilities of Proposition
5.4
can occur.

Example 5.5
.

(1)

Fix natural
n\geq{2}
and let
\mu\in\left]0,1\right[
be a (dependent on
n
) solution of
\mu^{n}+\mu^{3n+1}+\mu^{-3n-2}=\mu^{-n}+\mu^{-3n-1}+\mu^{3n+2}
. Next, define
F=\operatorname{diag}(\mu^{n/2},\mu^{(3n+1)/2},\mu^{-(3n+2)/2})
and consider the associated unitary quantum group
\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}
. By our choice of
\mu
and
F
we have
\lambda=1
and
\operatorname{Sp}(\uprho_{\alpha})=\{\mu^{n},\mu^{3n+1},\mu^{-3n-2}\}
. From the results discussed above we see that
\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
is a factor of type
\mathrm{III}_{\mu}
, hence
T(\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}))=\tfrac{2\pi}{\log{\mu}}\mathbb{Z}
and since
\operatorname{gcd}(2n,6n+2,6n+4)=2
, the reasoning in the proof of Proposition
5.4
gives
\Mod(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})=\tfrac{\pi}{\log{\mu}}\mathbb{Z}
. Finally Theorem
5.2
gives
\TtauInn(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})=\tfrac{2\pi}{(2n+1)\log{\mu}}\mathbb{Z}
.

(2)

Fix a natural
n\in\mathbb{N}
and let, as in the previous example,
\mu\in\left]0,1\right[
be a (dependent on
n
) solution of the equation
\mu^{n+1/2}+\mu^{3n+5/2}+\mu^{-(3n+7/2)}=\mu^{-(n+1/2)}+\mu^{-(3n+5/2)}+\mu^{3n+7/2}
. Next, set
F=\operatorname{diag}(\mu^{(n+1/2)/2},\mu^{(3n+5/2)/2},\mu^{-(3n+7/2)/2})
. As in example (
1
), we have
\lambda=1
and
\operatorname{Sp}(\uprho_{\alpha})=\{\mu^{n+1/2},\mu^{3n+5/2},\mu^{-(3n+7/2)}\}
. Consequently
\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
is a factor of type
\mathrm{III}_{\mu}
and
T(\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}))=\tfrac{2\pi}{\log{\mu}}\mathbb{Z}
. Furthermore
\operatorname{gcd}(2n+1,6n+5,6n+7)=1
, and hence the proof of Proposition
5.4
gives
\Mod(\widehat{\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}})=\tfrac{2\pi}{\log{\mu}}\mathbb{Z}
. Theorem
5.2
shows
\TtauInn(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})=\tfrac{2\pi}{(2n+2)\log{\mu}}\mathbb{Z}
.

(3)

Take
x>1
which is not an algebraic number and let
\kappa=\sqrt{\tfrac{2+1/x}{2+x}}\in\left]0,1\right[
, so that
2\kappa+\kappa{x}=\tfrac{2}{\kappa}+\tfrac{1}{\kappa{x}}
. Next, define
F=\operatorname{diag}(\sqrt{\kappa},\sqrt{\kappa},\sqrt{\kappa{x}})
and consider the associated quantum group
\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}
. We have
\lambda=1
and
\operatorname{Sp}(\uprho_{\alpha})=\{\kappa,\kappa{x}\}
. Consequently, the multiplicative group generated by
\operatorname{Sp}(\uprho_{\alpha}\otimes\uprho_{\alpha})
is the group generated by
\kappa^{2},x
which is easily seen to be dense in
\mathbb{R}_{>0}
as
x
is assumed to be a transcendental number (c.f.
[
23
, Example 5.27]
). Consequently
\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
is a factor of type
\mathrm{III}_{1}
and Proposition
5.4
gives
\Mod(\widehat{\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}})=\{0\}
. Since
\operatorname{Sp}(\uprho_{\alpha}\otimes\uprho_{\alpha}^{-1})=\{1,x,x^{-1}\}
, Theorem
5.2
gives
\TtauInn(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})=\tfrac{2\pi}{\log{x}}\mathbb{Z}
.

6.
i.c.c.-type conditions

Given a locally compact quantum group
\mathbb{G}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
we denote by
\Delta{G}^{(n)}
the map

(\underbrace{\mathrm{id}\otimes\dotsm\otimes\mathrm{id}}_{n-1}\otimes\Delta{G})\circ\dotsm\circ(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta{G})\circ\Delta{G}\colon\Linf(\mathbb{G})\longrightarrow\underbrace{\Linf(\mathbb{G})\overset{\rule[-1.0pt]{5.0pt}{0.5pt}}{\otimes}\dotsm\overset{\rule[-1.0pt]{5.0pt}{0.5pt}}{\otimes}\Linf(\mathbb{G})}_{n+1}.

(cf.
[
36
, p. 5]
,
[
1
, p. 57]
as well as Section
4.1
). In particular
\Delta{G}^{(1)}=\Delta{G}
.

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
\mathbb{G}
be a locally compact quantum group and assume that

\Delta{G}^{(n)}\bigl(\Linf(\mathbb{G})\bigr)^{\prime}\cap\underbrace{\Linf(\mathbb{G})\overset{\rule[-1.0pt]{5.0pt}{0.5pt}}{\otimes}\dotsm\overset{\rule[-1.0pt]{5.0pt}{0.5pt}}{\otimes}\Linf(\mathbb{G})}_{n+1}=\mathbb{C}\mathds{1}

for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Then
\Linf(\mathbb{G})
is a factor.

Proof.

Take
z\in\cZ(\Linf(\mathbb{G}))
. Then
z\otimes\mathds{1}^{\otimes{n}}
commutes with the range of
\Delta{G}^{(n)}
and hence
z\otimes\mathds{1}^{\otimes{n}}\in\mathbb{C}\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}
which implies
z\in\mathbb{C}\mathds{1}
.
∎

For a discrete group
\Gamma
the group von Neumann algebra
\operatorname{\mathnormal{L}}(\Gamma)
of
\Gamma
is the von Neumann algebra of operators on
\ell^{2}(\Gamma)
generated by the operators of the left regular representation
\{\lambda_{t}\,|\,t\in\Gamma\}
. The dual
\widehat{\Gamma}
of
\Gamma
is by definition the compact quantum group
\mathbb{G}
such that
\Linf(\mathbb{G})=\operatorname{\mathnormal{L}}(\Gamma)
with the unique comultiplication mapping
\lambda_{t}
to
\lambda_{t}\otimes\lambda_{t}
(
t\in\Gamma
).

Proposition 6.2
.

Let
\Gamma
be a discrete group. Then the following are equivalent:

(1)

\Gamma
is i.c.c.,

(2)

\operatorname{\mathnormal{L}}(\Gamma)
is a factor,

(3)

\Delta_{\widehat{\Gamma}}^{(n)}\bigl(\operatorname{\mathnormal{L}}(\Gamma)\bigr)^{\prime}\cap\underbrace{\operatorname{\mathnormal{L}}(\Gamma)\overset{\rule[-1.0pt]{5.0pt}{0.5pt}}{\otimes}\dotsm\overset{\rule[-1.0pt]{5.0pt}{0.5pt}}{\otimes}\operatorname{\mathnormal{L}}(\Gamma)}_{n+1}=\mathbb{C}\mathds{1}
for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

(4)

\Delta_{\widehat{\Gamma}}^{(n)}\bigl(\operatorname{\mathnormal{L}}(\Gamma)\bigr)^{\prime}\cap\underbrace{\operatorname{\mathnormal{L}}(\Gamma)\overset{\rule[-1.0pt]{5.0pt}{0.5pt}}{\otimes}\dotsm\overset{\rule[-1.0pt]{5.0pt}{0.5pt}}{\otimes}\operatorname{\mathnormal{L}}(\Gamma)}_{n+1}=\mathbb{C}\mathds{1}
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

Equivalence of (
1
) and (
2
) is well-known, (
4
)
\Rightarrow
(
3
) is clear, while (
3
)
\Rightarrow
(
2
) is contained in Proposition
6.1
.

For (
1
)
\Rightarrow
(
4
) take
x\in\underbrace{\operatorname{\mathnormal{L}}(\Gamma)\overset{\rule[-1.0pt]{5.0pt}{0.5pt}}{\otimes}\dotsm\overset{\rule[-1.0pt]{5.0pt}{0.5pt}}{\otimes}\operatorname{\mathnormal{L}}(\Gamma)}_{n+1}
and write
x
as the
\|\cdot\|_{2}
-convergent series

x=\sum\limits_{a_{1},\dotsc,a_{n+1}\in\Gamma}x_{a_{1},\dotsc,a_{n+1}}\lambda_{a_{1}}\otimes\dotsm\otimes\lambda_{a_{n+1}}.

If
x
commutes with
\Delta_{\widehat{\Gamma}}^{(n)}(\lambda_{c})
for all
c
then

\displaystyle\sum_{a_{1},\dotsc,a_{n+1}\in\Gamma}x_{a_{1},\dotsc,a_{n+1}}\lambda_{a_{1}}\otimes\dotsm

\displaystyle\otimes\lambda_{a_{n+1}}=x=(\lambda_{c}\otimes\dotsm\otimes\lambda_{c})x(\lambda_{c}\otimes\dotsm\otimes\lambda_{c})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{a_{1},\dotsc,a_{n+1}\in\Gamma}x_{a_{1},\dotsc,a_{n+1}}\lambda_{c{a_{1}}c^{-1}}\otimes\dotsm\otimes\lambda_{c{a_{n+1}}c^{-1}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{a_{1},\dotsc,a_{n+1}\in\Gamma}x_{c^{-1}a_{1}c,\dotsc,c^{-1}a_{n+1}c}\lambda_{a_{1}}\otimes\dotsm\otimes\lambda_{a_{n+1}}.

Now if additionally
x\not\in\mathbb{C}\mathds{1}
then there exists an
(n+1)
-tuple
(s_{1},\dotsc,s_{n+1})\neq(e,\dotsc,e)
with
x_{s_{1},\dotsc,s_{n+1}}\neq{0}
. Let
k
be such that
s_{k}\neq{e}
. Then the conjugacy class of
s_{k}
is infinite. Consequently there are infinitely many
(n+1)
-tuples
(a_{1},\dotsc,a_{n+1})
such that
(c^{-1}a_{1}c,\dotsc,c^{-1}a_{n+1}c)=(s_{1},\dotsc,s_{n+1})
for some
c
(namely take
a_{k}
from the conjugacy class of
s_{k}
, so that
a_{k}=cs_{k}c^{-1}
for some
c
, and put
a_{l}=cs_{l}c^{-1}
for the remaining
l
). But this leads to the contradiction

+\infty>\|x\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{p_{1},\dotsc,p_{n+1}\in\Gamma}|x_{p_{1},\dotsc,p_{n+1}}|^{2}=+\infty.

It follows that
x
must be proportional to
\mathds{1}
.
∎

Definition 6.3
.

Let
\mathbbl{\Gamma}
be a discrete quantum group. We say that
\mathbbl{\Gamma}
is
n
-i.c.c. if

\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbbl{\Gamma}}}^{(n)}\bigl(\Linf(\widehat{\mathbbl{\Gamma}})\bigr)^{\prime}\cap\underbrace{\Linf(\widehat{\mathbbl{\Gamma}})\overset{\rule[-1.0pt]{5.0pt}{0.5pt}}{\otimes}\dotsm\overset{\rule[-1.0pt]{5.0pt}{0.5pt}}{\otimes}\Linf(\widehat{\mathbbl{\Gamma}})}_{n+1}=\mathbb{C}\mathds{1}.

We immediately see that for a classical discrete group
\Gamma
we have that
\Gamma
is an i.c.c. group if and only if
\Gamma
is
1
-i.c.c., which happens if and only if
\Gamma
is
n
-i.c.c. for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Remark 6.4
.

Let
\mathbbl{\Gamma}
be a discrete quantum group. If
y\in\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbbl{\Gamma}}}^{(n-1)}\bigl(\Linf(\widehat{\mathbbl{\Gamma}})\bigr)^{\prime}\cap\underbrace{\Linf(\widehat{\mathbbl{\Gamma}})\overset{\rule[-1.0pt]{5.0pt}{0.5pt}}{\otimes}\dotsm\overset{\rule[-1.0pt]{5.0pt}{0.5pt}}{\otimes}\Linf(\widehat{\mathbbl{\Gamma}})}_{n}
for some
n\geq{2}
then
\bigl(\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbbl{\Gamma}}}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes(n-1)}\bigr)y
belongs to
\Delta_{\widehat{\mathbbl{\Gamma}}}^{(n)}\bigl(\Linf(\widehat{\mathbbl{\Gamma}})\bigr)^{\prime}\cap\underbrace{\Linf(\widehat{\mathbbl{\Gamma}})\overset{\rule[-1.0pt]{5.0pt}{0.5pt}}{\otimes}\dotsm\overset{\rule[-1.0pt]{5.0pt}{0.5pt}}{\otimes}\Linf(\widehat{\mathbbl{\Gamma}})}_{n+1}
which shows that if
\mathbbl{\Gamma}
is
n
-i.c.c. then it is also
m
-i.c.c. for all
m<n
.

Theorem 6.5
.

Assume that
\mathbb{G}
is a second countable compact quantum group such that
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
is
1
-i.c.c. and
\TtauInn(\mathbb{G})=\mathbb{R}
. Then
\mathbb{G}
is of Kac type.

Proof.

Write
\tau^{\mathbb{G}}_{t}=\Ad(b_{0}^{\mathrm{i}{t}})
for a strictly positive, self-adjoint operator
b_{0}
affiliated with
\Linf(\mathbb{G})
(the existence of
b_{0}
follows from
[
16
, Theorem 0.1]
and Stone’s theorem, cf. e.g. the proof of Theorem
3.3
). Using
\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}\circ\tau^{\mathbb{G}}_{t}=(\tau{G}_{t}\otimes\tau{G}_{t})\circ\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}
we obtain

(b_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}{t}}\otimes{b_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}{t}}})\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}(b_{0}^{\mathrm{i}{t}})\in\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}\bigl(\Linf(\mathbb{G})\bigr)^{\prime}\cap\Linf(\mathbb{G})\overset{\rule[-1.0pt]{5.0pt}{0.5pt}}{\otimes}\Linf(\mathbb{G})=\mathbb{C}\mathds{1},

so defining
z_{t}\in\mathbb{T}
by

(6.1)

(b_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}{t}}\otimes{b_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}{t}}})\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}(b_{0}^{\mathrm{i}{t}})=z_{t}\mathds{1}

we obtain a continuous map
\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{T}
. This map is a homomorphism, since

\displaystyle z_{t+s}=(b_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}(t+s)}\otimes{b_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}(t+s)}})\Delta{G}(b_{0}^{\mathrm{i}(t+s)})

\displaystyle=(b_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}{t}}\otimes{b_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}{t}}})\bigl((b_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}{s}}\otimes{b_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}{s}}})\Delta{G}(b_{0}^{\mathrm{i}{s}})\bigr)\Delta{G}(b_{0}^{\mathrm{i}{t}})

\displaystyle=(b_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}{t}}\otimes{b_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}{t}}})\Delta{G}(b_{0}^{\mathrm{i}{t}})\bigl((b_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}{s}}\otimes{b_{0}^{-\mathrm{i}{s}}})\Delta{G}(b_{0}^{\mathrm{i}{s}})\bigr)=z_{t}z_{s}.

It follows that we can write
z_{t}=\lambda^{\mathrm{i}{t}}
for some
\lambda>0
. Define
b=\lambda{b_{0}}
. Then we still have
\tau_{t}=\Ad(b^{\mathrm{i}{t}})
and by (
6.1
)

\Delta{G}(b^{\mathrm{i}{t}})=\lambda^{\mathrm{i}{t}}\Delta{G}(b_{0}^{\mathrm{i}{t}})=\lambda^{\mathrm{i}{t}}z_{t}(b_{0}^{\mathrm{i}{t}}\otimes{b_{0}^{\mathrm{i}{t}}})=(\lambda^{\mathrm{i}{t}}b_{0}^{\mathrm{i}{t}}\otimes\lambda^{\mathrm{i}{t}}b_{0}^{\mathrm{i}{t}})=(b^{\mathrm{i}{t}}\otimes{b^{\mathrm{i}{t}}}).

Fix
t\in\mathbb{R}
and for
n\in\mathbb{N}
define a bounded measurable function
f_{n}\colon\left]0,+\infty\right[\to\mathbb{C}
by
f_{n}(x)=\int\limits_{t-\frac{1}{n}}^{t+\frac{1}{n}}x^{\mathrm{i}{s}}\,\mathrm{d}{s}
. Then

f_{n}(b)=\int\limits_{t-\frac{1}{n}}^{t+\frac{1}{n}}b^{\mathrm{i}{s}}\,\mathrm{d}{s}

and

\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}\bigl(f_{n}(b)\bigr)=\int\limits_{t-\frac{1}{n}}^{t+\frac{1}{n}}\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}(b^{\mathrm{i}{s}})\,\mathrm{d}{s}=\int\limits_{t-\frac{1}{n}}^{t+\frac{1}{n}}(b^{\mathrm{i}{s}}\otimes{b^{\mathrm{i}{s}}})\,\mathrm{d}{s}.

Applying
{\boldsymbol{h}}{G}\otimes\mathrm{id}
and using right invariance of
{\boldsymbol{h}}{G}
gives

\Biggl(\,\int\limits_{t-\frac{1}{n}}^{t+\frac{1}{n}}{\boldsymbol{h}}{G}(b^{\mathrm{i}{s}})\,\mathrm{d}{s}\Biggr)\mathds{1}={\boldsymbol{h}}{G}\bigl(f_{n}(b)\bigr)\mathds{1}=({\boldsymbol{h}}{G}\otimes\mathrm{id})\Delta_{\mathbb{G}}\bigl(f_{n}(b)\bigr)=\int\limits_{t-\frac{1}{n}}^{t+\frac{1}{n}}{\boldsymbol{h}}{G}(b^{\mathrm{i}{s}})b^{\mathrm{i}{s}}\,\mathrm{d}{s}.

Now multiplying the above by
2n
and passing to the limit
n\to\infty
gives

{\boldsymbol{h}}{G}(b^{\mathrm{i}{t}})\mathds{1}={\boldsymbol{h}}{G}(b^{\mathrm{i}{t}})b^{\mathrm{i}{t}},\qquad{t}\in\mathbb{R}.

Since
{\boldsymbol{h}}{G}(b^{\mathrm{i}0})={\boldsymbol{h}}{G}(\mathds{1})=1
and
t\mapsto{\boldsymbol{h}}{G}(b^{\mathrm{i}{t}})
is continuous, there exists
\varepsilon>0
such that
{\boldsymbol{h}}{G}(b^{\mathrm{i}{t}})\neq{0}
for
|t|<\varepsilon
. Consequently
b^{\mathrm{i}{t}}=\mathds{1}
for such
t
. But then for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
we have
b^{\mathrm{i}{m}t}=(b^{\mathrm{i}{t}})^{m}=\mathds{1}^{m}=\mathds{1}
, and hence
b=\mathds{1}
and
\mathbb{G}
is of Kac type.
∎

6.1.
Example:
\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}

In this section we show that for an appropriate choice of the invertible matrix
F
(this is made more precise in Theorem
6.7
below) the quantum group
\widehat{\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}}
satisfies the
n
-i.c.c. condition. This, in particular, gives an alternative proof that Conjecture
1
holds for
\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}
, but is meant more as an illustration of the results of Section
6
. As in Section
5.3
we adapt the ideas and proofs contained in
[
10
, Appendix]
. We retain the notation introduced in Section
5.3
. To further ease the notation, we also denote
\Delta_{\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}}
simply by
\Delta
and identify
a\in\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
with its image in
\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})
.

Fix
n\geq{1}
,
x\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}\star\mathbb{Z}_{+}
and define a bounded map
V^{\Delta,n}_{x}\colon\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\otimes(n+1)}\to\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\mathscr{K}_{x}
by setting

V^{\Delta,n}_{x}(a)=(\Delta^{(n)}\otimes\mathrm{id})(u^{x})(a\otimes\mathds{1})(\Delta^{(n)}\otimes\mathrm{id})(u^{x})^{*},\qquad{a}\in\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\overset{\rule[-1.0pt]{5.0pt}{0.5pt}}{\otimes}(n+1)}

on a dense set. We will shortly show that
V_{x}^{\Delta,n}
as defined above is indeed bounded, hence extends uniquely to the whole space
\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\otimes(n+1)}
.

It seems that map
V_{x}^{\Delta,n}
, unlike
V_{x}^{\tau,t}
from Section
5.3
, is not isometric – it is however close to being isometric when
\uprho_{x}
is close to
\mathds{1}
, which we will now quantify. Take any
a,b\in\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\overset{\rule[-1.0pt]{5.0pt}{0.5pt}}{\otimes}(n+1)}
. Then using equation (
5.3
) and employing the leg-numbering notation
[\,\cdot\,]_{k\,l}
we have

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!{\left\langle{V^{\Delta,n}_{x}(a)}\,\vline\,V^{\Delta,n}_{x}(b)\right\rangle}=({\boldsymbol{h}}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\omega_{x})\bigl((\Delta^{(n)}\otimes\mathrm{id})(u^{x})(a^{*}b\otimes\mathds{1})(\Delta^{(n)}\otimes\mathrm{id})({u^{x}}^{*})\bigr)

\displaystyle=\Bigl({\boldsymbol{h}}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\tfrac{\Tr}{\qdim{x}}\Bigr)\bigl((\Delta^{(n)}\otimes\mathrm{id})(u^{x})(a^{*}b\otimes\mathds{1})(\Delta^{(n)}\otimes\mathrm{id})({u^{x}}^{*})(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{-1})\bigr)

\displaystyle=\Bigl({\boldsymbol{h}}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\tfrac{\Tr}{\qdim{x}}\Bigr)\bigl((a^{*}b\otimes\mathds{1})(\Delta^{(n)}\otimes\mathrm{id})({u^{x}}^{*})(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{-1})(\sigma^{{\boldsymbol{h}}^{\otimes(n+1)}}_{-\mathrm{i}}\otimes\mathrm{id})(\Delta^{(n)}\otimes\mathrm{id})(u^{x})\bigr)

\displaystyle=\Bigl({\boldsymbol{h}}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\tfrac{\Tr}{\qdim{x}}\Bigr)\Bigl((a^{*}b\otimes\mathds{1})\bigl[{u^{x}}^{*}\bigr]_{n+1\,n+2}\dotsm\bigl[{u^{x}}^{*}\bigr]_{1\,n+2}(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{-1})

\displaystyle\qquad\bigl[(\sigma^{{\boldsymbol{h}}}_{-\mathrm{i}}\otimes\mathrm{id})(u^{x})\bigr]_{1\,n+2}\dotsm\bigl[(\sigma^{{\boldsymbol{h}}}_{-\mathrm{i}}\otimes\mathrm{id})(u^{x})\bigr]_{n+1\,n+2}\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\Bigl({\boldsymbol{h}}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\tfrac{\Tr}{\qdim{x}}\Bigr)\Bigl((a^{*}b\otimes\mathds{1})\bigl[{u^{x}}^{*}\bigr]_{n+1\,n+2}\dotsm\bigl[{u^{x}}^{*}\bigr]_{1\,n+2}(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{-1})

\displaystyle\qquad(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x})[u^{x}]_{1\,n+2}\bigl((\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{2})[u^{x}]_{2\,n+2}\bigr)\dotsm\bigl((\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{2})[u^{x}]_{n+1\,n+2}\bigr)(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x})\Bigr)

\displaystyle=({\boldsymbol{h}}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\omega_{x})\Bigl((a^{*}b\otimes\mathds{1})\bigl[{u^{x}}^{*}\bigr]_{n+1\,n+2}\dotsm\bigl[{u^{x}}^{*}\bigr]_{2\,n+2}(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad\bigl([u^{x}]_{2\,n+2}(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{2})\bigr)\dotsm\bigl([u^{x}]_{n+1\,n+2}(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{2})\bigr)\Bigr).

Consequently, writing
J
for
J_{{\boldsymbol{h}}^{\otimes\,(n+1)}\otimes\,\omega_{x}}
we obtain

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\bigl|{\left\langle{V^{\Delta,n}_{x}(a)}\,\vline\,V^{\Delta,n}_{x}(b)\right\rangle}-{\left\langle{a}\,\vline\,b\right\rangle}\bigr|

\displaystyle=\Bigl|({\boldsymbol{h}}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\omega_{x})\Bigl((a^{*}b\otimes\mathds{1})\Bigl(\bigl[{u^{x}}^{*}\bigr]_{n+1\,n+2}\dotsm\bigl[{u^{x}}^{*}\bigr]_{2\,n+2}(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\bigl([u^{x}]_{2\,n+2}(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{2})\bigr)\dotsm\bigl([u^{x}]_{n+1\,n+2}(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{2})\bigr)-\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\mathds{1}\Bigr)\Bigr)\Bigr|

\displaystyle=\Bigl|\Bigl\langle{a}\otimes\mathds{1}\Big|(b\otimes\mathds{1})\Bigl(\bigl[{u^{x}}^{*}\bigr]_{n+1\,n+2}\dotsm\bigl[{u^{x}}^{*}\bigr]_{2\,n+2}(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\bigl([u^{x}]_{2\,n+2}(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{2})\bigr)\dotsm\bigl([u^{x}]_{n+1\,n+2}(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{2})\bigr)-\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\mathds{1}\Bigr)\Bigr\rangle\Bigr|

\displaystyle=\Bigl|\Bigl\langle{a}\otimes\mathds{1}\Big|J\sigma^{{\boldsymbol{h}}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\omega_{x}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{i}\;\!\!/\;\!\!2}}\Bigl(\bigl[{u^{x}}^{*}\bigr]_{n+1\,n+2}\dotsm\bigl[{u^{x}}^{*}\bigr]_{2\,n+2}(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\bigl([u^{x}]_{2\,n+2}(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{2})\bigr)\dotsm\bigl([u^{x}]_{n+1\,n+2}(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{2})\bigr)-\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\mathds{1}\Bigr)^{*}J(b\otimes\mathds{1})\Bigr\rangle\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\|a\otimes\mathds{1}\|_{2}\Bigl\|\sigma_{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{i}\;\!\!/\;\!\!2}}^{{\boldsymbol{h}}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\omega_{x}}\Bigl(\bigl[{u^{x}}^{*}\bigr]_{n+1\,n+2}\dotsm\bigl[{u^{x}}^{*}\bigr]_{2\,n+2}(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\bigl([u^{x}]_{2\,n+2}(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{2})\bigr)\dotsm\bigl([u^{x}]_{n+1\,n+2}(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{2})\bigr)-\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\mathds{1}\Bigr)\Bigr\|\|b\otimes\mathds{1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|a\|_{2}\Bigl\|\sigma_{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{i}\;\!\!/\;\!\!2}}^{{\boldsymbol{h}}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\omega_{x}}\Bigl(\bigl[{u^{x}}^{*}\bigr]_{n+1\,n+2}\dotsm\bigl[{u^{x}}^{*}\bigr]_{2\,n+2}(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\bigl([u^{x}]_{2\,n+2}(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{2})\bigr)\dotsm\bigl([u^{x}]_{n+1\,n+2}(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{2})\bigr)-\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\mathds{1}\Bigr)\Bigr\|\|b\|_{2}.

This shows that
V^{\Delta,n}_{x}
extends to a bounded map. Since
\sigma_{t}^{{\boldsymbol{h}}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\omega_{x}}=(\sigma^{\boldsymbol{h}}_{t})^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\sigma^{\omega_{x}}_{t}
and

\sigma^{\omega_{x}}_{t}(T)=\uprho_{x}^{-\mathrm{i}{t}}T\uprho_{x}^{\mathrm{i}{t}},\qquad{T}\in\B(\mathscr{H}_{x}),\>t\in\mathbb{R},

we can continue the calculation:

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\bigl|{\left\langle{V^{\Delta,n}_{x}(a)}\,\vline\,V^{\Delta,n}_{x}(b)\right\rangle}-{\left\langle{a}\,\vline\,b\right\rangle}\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\|a\|_{2}\|b\|_{2}\Bigl\|(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}})

\displaystyle\qquad\Bigl(\bigl[(\sigma^{{\boldsymbol{h}}}_{-\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{i}\;\!\!/\;\!\!2}}\otimes\mathrm{id})(u^{x})\bigr]_{n+1\,n+2}^{*}\dotsm\bigl[(\sigma^{{\boldsymbol{h}}}_{-\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{i}\;\!\!/\;\!\!2}}\otimes\mathrm{id})(u^{x})\bigr]_{2\,n+2}^{*}(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad\>\>\,\bigl[(\sigma^{{\boldsymbol{h}}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{i}\;\!\!/\;\!\!2}}\otimes\mathrm{id})(u^{x})(\mathds{1}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{2})\bigr]_{2\,n+2}\dotsm\bigl[(\sigma^{{\boldsymbol{h}}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{i}\;\!\!/\;\!\!2}}\otimes\mathrm{id})(u^{x})(\mathds{1}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{2})\bigr]_{n+1\,n+2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\mathds{1}\Bigr)(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{-\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}})\Bigr\|

\displaystyle\leq\|a\|_{2}\|b\|_{2}\Bigl\|(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}})

\displaystyle\qquad\Bigl(\bigl[(\mathds{1}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}}){u^{x}}^{*}(\mathds{1}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}})\bigr]_{n+1\,n+2}\dotsm\bigl[(\mathds{1}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}}){u^{x}}^{*}(\mathds{1}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}})\bigr]_{2\,n+2}(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad\>\>\,\bigl[(\mathds{1}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{-\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}})u^{x}(\mathds{1}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{\scriptscriptstyle{3\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}})\bigr]_{2\,n+2}\dotsm\bigl[(\mathds{1}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{-\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}})u^{x}(\mathds{1}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{\scriptscriptstyle{3\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}})\bigr]_{n+1\,n+2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\mathds{1}\Bigr)(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{-\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}})\Bigr\|

\displaystyle=\|a\|_{2}\|b\|_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\Bigl\|\Bigl((\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x})\bigl[{u^{x}}^{*}\bigr]_{n+1\,n+2}\Bigr)\dotsm\Bigl((\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x})\bigl[{u^{x}}^{*}\bigr]_{2\,n+2}\Bigr)(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\bigl([u^{x}]_{2\,n+2}(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x})\bigr)\dotsm\bigl([u^{x}]_{n+1\,n+2}(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x})\bigr)-\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\mathds{1}\Bigr\|.

The last norm can be estimated as follows

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\Bigl\|\Bigl((\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x})\bigl[{u^{x}}^{*}\bigr]_{n+1\,n+2}\Bigr)\dotsm\Bigl((\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x})\bigl[{u^{x}}^{*}\bigr]_{2\,n+2}\Bigr)(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\bigl([u^{x}]_{2\,n+2}(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x})\bigr)\dotsm\bigl([u^{x}]_{n+1\,n+2}(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x})\bigr)-\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\mathds{1}\Bigr\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\uprho_{x}^{2}-\mathds{1}\|\|\uprho_{x}\|^{2n}+\Bigl\|\bigl((\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x})\bigl[{u^{x}}^{*}\bigr]_{n+1\,n+2}\bigr)\dotsm\bigl((\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x})\bigl[{u^{x}}^{*}\bigr]_{3\,n+2}\bigr)(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\bigl([u^{x}]_{3\,n+2}(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x})\bigr)\dotsm\bigl([u^{x}]_{n+1\,n+2}(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x})\bigr)-\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\mathds{1}\Bigr\|

and proceeding in this way we obtain

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\Bigl\|\Bigl((\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x})\bigl[{u^{x}}^{*}\bigr]_{n+1\,n+2}\Bigr)\dotsm\Bigl((\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x})\bigl[{u^{x}}^{*}\bigr]_{2\,n+2}\Bigr)(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\bigl([u^{x}]_{2\,n+2}(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x})\bigr)\dotsm\bigl([u^{x}]_{n+1\,n+2}(\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x})\bigr)-\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\mathds{1}\Bigr\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\uprho_{x}^{2}-\mathds{1}\|\|\uprho_{x}\|^{2n}+\dotsm+\|\uprho_{x}^{2}-\mathds{1}\|\|\uprho_{x}\|^{2}+\bigl\|\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\uprho_{x}^{2}-\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\mathds{1}\bigr\|=\|\uprho_{x}^{2}-\mathds{1}\|\tfrac{\|\uprho_{x}\|^{2(n+1)}-1}{\|\uprho_{x}\|^{2}-1}

(where the last equality holds when
\uprho_{x}\neq\mathds{1}
). This gives us the following bound

(6.2)

\Bigl|{\left\langle{V^{\Delta,n}_{x}(a)}\,\vline\,V^{\Delta,n}_{x}(b)\right\rangle}-{\left\langle{a}\,\vline\,b\right\rangle}\Bigr|\leq\|a\|_{2}\|b\|_{2}\|\uprho_{x}^{2}-\mathds{1}\|\tfrac{\|\uprho_{x}\|^{2(n+1)}-1}{\|\uprho_{x}\|^{2}-1}

for
a,b\in\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\overset{\rule[-1.0pt]{5.0pt}{0.5pt}}{\otimes}(n+1)}
, provided
\uprho_{x}\neq\mathds{1}
. It will be useful in the proof of the next proposition based on
[
10
, Proposition 32]
. The proof is similar as in
[
10
]
, but somewhat complicated by the facts that the operators
V^{\Delta,n}_{x}
(
x\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}\star\mathbb{Z}_{+}
) are not isometric and we work in
(n+1)
-fold tensor product space.

Let us introduce

D_{x,n}=\begin{cases}\|\uprho_{x}^{2}-\mathds{1}\|\tfrac{\|\uprho_{x}\|^{2(n+1)}-1}{\|\uprho_{x}\|^{2}-1}&\uprho_{x}\neq\mathds{1},\\
0&\uprho_{x}=\mathds{1}\end{cases},\qquad{x}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}\star\mathbb{Z}_{+}

and put

(6.3)

D_{n}=\max\bigl\{D_{\alpha\beta,n},D_{\beta\alpha,n},D_{\alpha^{2}\beta,n}\bigr\}.

It follows from (
6.2
) that
\|V^{\Delta,n}_{x}\|\leq(1+D_{x,n})^{\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}}
. Next, for
x\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}\star\mathbb{Z}_{+}\setminus\{\varepsilon\}
introduce subspaces of
\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})

\displaystyle C_{x}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{span}\bigl\{(\mathrm{id}\otimes\omega)u^{x}\,\bigr|\bigl.\,\omega\in\B(\mathscr{H}_{x})_{*}\bigr\},

\displaystyle L_{x}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\overline{span}}\bigcup_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}\star\mathbb{Z}_{+}}C_{xz},\qquad{L_{\varepsilon}}=\mathbb{C}\mathds{1}.

Given an operator
a\in\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\overset{\rule[-1.0pt]{5.0pt}{0.5pt}}{\otimes}(n+1)}\,(n\in\mathbb{N})
we will decompose it as follows

a=\sum_{x_{1},\dotsc,x_{n+1}\in\{\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta\}}a_{x_{1},\dotsc,x_{n+1}}

with respect to the decomposition

\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\otimes(n+1)}=\bigoplus_{x_{1},\dotsc,x_{n+1}\in\{\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta\}}L_{x_{1}}\otimes\dotsm\otimes{L_{x_{n+1}}}.

Proposition 6.6
.

Take
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
a\in\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\overset{\rule[-1.0pt]{5.0pt}{0.5pt}}{\otimes}(n+1)}
. If
D_{n}<1-\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}
, then for any
1\leq{k}\leq{n}
we have

\displaystyle\biggl\|a-

\displaystyle\sum_{x_{i}\in\{\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta\},\>i\neq{k}}a_{x_{1},\dotsc,x_{k-1},\varepsilon,x_{k+1},\dotsc,x_{n+1}}\biggr\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{2(7-4D_{n})}{2(1-D_{n})^{2}-1}\Bigl(D_{n}\|a\|_{2}+(1+D_{n})^{\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}}\max_{x\in\{\alpha\beta,\beta\alpha,\alpha^{2}\beta\}}\bigl\|a\otimes\mathds{1}-V^{\Delta,n}_{x}(a)\bigr\|_{2}\Bigr).

Proof.

Without loss of generality assume that
\|a\|_{2}=1
and set

M=\max_{x\in\{\alpha\beta,\beta\alpha,\alpha^{2}\beta\}}\bigl\|a\otimes\mathds{1}-V^{\Delta,n}_{x}(a)\bigr\|_{2}.

Observe that for
x,y\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}\star\mathbb{Z}_{+}

\displaystyle V^{\Delta,n}_{x}\bigl(\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\otimes(k-1)}

\displaystyle\otimes{C_{y}}\otimes\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\otimes(n+1-k)}\bigr)

\displaystyle\subset\operatorname{\overline{span}}\bigcup_{z\subset{x}\text{\tiny$\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.40282pt\hskip-3.40282pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\bigcirc$}$}\hskip-3.40282pt\hskip-2.70836pt\raisebox{-0.85208pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\top$}$}}\hskip-2.70836pt\hskip 3.40282pt}}$}{y}\text{\tiny$\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.40282pt\hskip-3.40282pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\bigcirc$}$}\hskip-3.40282pt\hskip-2.70836pt\raisebox{-0.85208pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\top$}$}}\hskip-2.70836pt\hskip 3.40282pt}}$}\overline{x}}\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\otimes(k-1)}\otimes{C_{z}}\otimes\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\otimes(n+1-k)}\otimes\mathscr{K}_{x}

and consequently

(6.4)

\displaystyle V^{\Delta,n}_{\beta\alpha}\bigl(\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\otimes(k-1)}\otimes{L_{\alpha}}\otimes\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\otimes(n+1-k)}\bigr)

\displaystyle\subset\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\otimes(k-1)}\otimes{L_{\beta\alpha}}\otimes\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\otimes(n+1-k)}\otimes\mathscr{K}_{\beta\alpha},

\displaystyle V^{\Delta,n}_{\alpha\beta}\bigl(\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\otimes(k-1)}\otimes{L_{\beta}}\otimes\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\otimes(n+1-k)}\bigr)

\displaystyle\subset\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\otimes(k-1)}\otimes{L_{\alpha\beta}}\otimes\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\otimes(n+1-k)}\otimes\mathscr{K}_{\alpha\beta},

\displaystyle V^{\Delta,n}_{\alpha^{2}\beta}\bigl(\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\otimes(k-1)}\otimes{L_{\beta}}\otimes\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\otimes(n+1-k)}\bigr)

\displaystyle\subset\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\otimes(k-1)}\otimes{L_{\alpha^{2}}}\otimes\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\otimes(n+1-k)}\otimes\mathscr{K}_{\alpha^{2}\beta}.

Indeed, if
w=\alpha{y}
for some
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}\star\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, then using fusion rules of
\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}
(equation (
3.6
)) we have
\beta\alpha\text{\tiny$\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.40282pt\hskip-3.40282pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\bigcirc$}$}\hskip-3.40282pt\hskip-2.70836pt\raisebox{-0.85208pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\top$}$}}\hskip-2.70836pt\hskip 3.40282pt}}$}{w}\text{\tiny$\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.40282pt\hskip-3.40282pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\bigcirc$}$}\hskip-3.40282pt\hskip-2.70836pt\raisebox{-0.85208pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\top$}$}}\hskip-2.70836pt\hskip 3.40282pt}}$}\overline{\beta\alpha}=\beta\alpha^{2}y\text{\tiny$\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.40282pt\hskip-3.40282pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\bigcirc$}$}\hskip-3.40282pt\hskip-2.70836pt\raisebox{-0.85208pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\top$}$}}\hskip-2.70836pt\hskip 3.40282pt}}$}\beta\alpha
and any
z\subset\beta\alpha\text{\tiny$\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.40282pt\hskip-3.40282pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\bigcirc$}$}\hskip-3.40282pt\hskip-2.70836pt\raisebox{-0.85208pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\top$}$}}\hskip-2.70836pt\hskip 3.40282pt}}$}{w}\text{\tiny$\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.40282pt\hskip-3.40282pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\bigcirc$}$}\hskip-3.40282pt\hskip-2.70836pt\raisebox{-0.85208pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\top$}$}}\hskip-2.70836pt\hskip 3.40282pt}}$}\overline{\beta\alpha}
has to begin with
\beta\alpha
. This shows the first inclusion in (
6.4
), the remaining ones can be argued analogously.

To ease the notation, for
x\in\{\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta\}
let us introduce

b_{x}=\sum_{x_{i}\in\{\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta\},\>i\neq{k}}a_{x_{1},\dotsc,x_{k-1},x,x_{k+1},\dotsc,x_{n+1}}\in\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\otimes(k-1)}\otimes{L_{x}}\otimes\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\otimes(n+1-k)},

so that
a=b_{\varepsilon}+b_{\alpha}+b_{\beta}
. Using (
6.4
) and (
6.2
), we obtain the estimate

(6.5a)

\displaystyle\bigl|\bigl\langle{b_{\beta}}\otimes\mathds{1}-V^{\Delta,n}_{\beta\alpha}(b_{\alpha})\big|V^{\Delta,n}_{\beta\alpha}(b_{\alpha})\bigr\rangle\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\bigl|\bigl\langle{a}\otimes\mathds{1}-V^{\Delta,n}_{\beta\alpha}(a)\big|V^{\Delta,n}_{\beta\alpha}(b_{\alpha})\bigr\rangle\bigr|+\bigl|\bigl\langle(b_{\varepsilon}+b_{\alpha})\otimes\mathds{1}-V^{\Delta,n}_{\beta\alpha}(b_{\varepsilon}+b_{\beta})\big|V^{\Delta,n}_{\beta\alpha}(b_{\alpha})\bigr\rangle\bigr|

\displaystyle=\bigl|\bigl\langle{a}\otimes\mathds{1}-V^{\Delta,n}_{\beta\alpha}(a)\big|V^{\Delta,n}_{\beta\alpha}(b_{\alpha})\bigr\rangle\bigr|+\bigl|\bigl\langle{V^{\Delta,n}_{\beta\alpha}}(b_{\varepsilon}+b_{\beta})\big|V^{\Delta,n}_{\beta\alpha}(b_{\alpha})\bigr\rangle\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|a\otimes\mathds{1}-V^{\Delta,n}_{\beta\alpha}(a)\bigr\|_{2}\bigl\|V^{\Delta,n}_{\beta\alpha}(b_{\alpha})\bigr\|+D_{\beta\alpha,n}\|b_{\varepsilon}+b_{\beta}\|_{2}\|b_{\alpha}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq{M}(1+D_{\beta\alpha,n})^{\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}}\|b_{\alpha}\|_{2}+D_{\beta\alpha,n}\|b_{\varepsilon}+b_{\beta}\|_{2}\|b_{\alpha}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq{M}(1+D_{n})^{\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}}\|a-b_{\varepsilon}\|_{2}+D_{n}\|a-b_{\varepsilon}\|_{2}

and similarly to (
6.5a
) we derive

(6.5b)

\bigl|\bigl\langle b_{\alpha}\otimes\mathds{1}-V^{\Delta,n}_{\alpha\beta}(b_{\beta})\big|V^{\Delta,n}_{\alpha\beta}(b_{\beta})\bigr\rangle\bigr|\leq{M}(1+D_{n})^{\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}}\|a-b_{\varepsilon}\|_{2}+D_{n}\|a-b_{\varepsilon}\|_{2}

as well as

(6.5c)

\bigl|\bigl\langle{b_{\alpha}}\otimes\mathds{1}-V^{\Delta,n}_{\alpha^{2}\beta}(b_{\beta})\big|V^{\Delta,n}_{\alpha^{2}\beta}(b_{\beta})\bigr\rangle\bigr|\leq{M}(1+D_{n})^{\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}}\|a-b_{\varepsilon}\|_{2}+D_{n}\|a-b_{\varepsilon}\|_{2}.

Using (
6.5a
) and again (
6.2
) we arrive at

(6.6)

\displaystyle\|b_{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\bigl\|b_{\beta}\otimes\mathds{1}-V^{\Delta,n}_{\beta\alpha}(b_{\alpha})+V^{\Delta,n}_{\beta\alpha}(b_{\alpha})\bigr\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\bigl\|b_{\beta}\otimes\mathds{1}-V^{\Delta,n}_{\beta\alpha}(b_{\alpha})\bigr\|^{2}+\bigl\|V^{\Delta,n}_{\beta\alpha}(b_{\alpha})\bigr\|^{2}_{2}-2\bigl|\bigl\langle{b_{\beta}}\otimes\mathds{1}-V^{\Delta,n}_{\beta\alpha}(b_{\alpha})\big|V^{\Delta,n}_{\beta\alpha}(b_{\alpha})\bigr\rangle\bigr|

\displaystyle\geq(1-D_{n})\|b_{\alpha}\|_{2}^{2}-2\bigl|\bigl\langle{b_{\beta}}\otimes\mathds{1}-V^{\Delta,n}_{\beta\alpha}(b_{\alpha})\big|V^{\Delta,n}_{\beta\alpha}(b_{\alpha})\bigr\rangle\bigr|

\displaystyle\geq(1-D_{n})\|b_{\alpha}\|_{2}^{2}-2\bigl(M(1+D_{n})^{\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}}+D_{n}\bigr)\|a-b_{\varepsilon}\|_{2}.

Define bounded operators

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{\Delta,n}_{\alpha\beta,12}

\displaystyle\colon\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\otimes(n+1)}\ni\xi\longmapsto\bigl(V^{\Delta,n}_{\alpha\beta}\xi\bigr)_{12}\in\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\mathscr{K}_{\alpha\beta}\otimes\mathscr{K}_{\alpha^{2}\beta},

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{\Delta,n}_{\alpha^{2}\beta,13}

\displaystyle\colon\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\otimes(n+1)}\ni\xi\longmapsto\bigl(V^{\Delta,n}_{\alpha^{2}\beta}\xi\bigr)_{13}\in\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\otimes(n+1)}\otimes\mathscr{K}_{\alpha\beta}\otimes\mathscr{K}_{\alpha^{2}\beta}.

It follows from (
6.4
) that the images of
\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\otimes(k-1)}\otimes{L_{\beta}}\otimes\Ltwo(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\otimes(n+1-k)}
under
\mathcal{V}^{\Delta,n}_{\alpha\beta,12}
and
\mathcal{V}^{\Delta,n}_{\alpha^{2}\beta,13}
are orthogonal. Using (
6.5b
) we obtain

\displaystyle\quad\;\bigl|\bigl\langle{b_{\alpha}}\otimes\mathds{1}\otimes\mathds{1}-\mathcal{V}^{\Delta,n}_{\alpha\beta,12}(b_{\beta})-\mathcal{V}^{\Delta,n}_{\alpha^{2}\beta,13}(b_{\beta})\big|\mathcal{V}^{\Delta,n}_{\alpha\beta,12}(b_{\beta})\bigr|=\bigl|\bigl\langle b_{\alpha}\otimes\mathds{1}\otimes\mathds{1}-\mathcal{V}^{\Delta,n}_{\alpha\beta,12}(b_{\beta})\big|\mathcal{V}^{\Delta,n}_{\alpha\beta,12}(b_{\beta})\bigr\rangle\bigr|

\displaystyle=\bigl|\bigl\langle{b_{\alpha}}\otimes\mathds{1}-V^{\Delta,n}_{\alpha\beta}(b_{\beta})\big|V^{\Delta,n}_{\alpha\beta}(b_{\beta})\bigr\rangle\bigr|\leq\bigl(M(1+D_{n})^{\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}}+D_{n}\bigr)\|a-b_{\varepsilon}\|_{2}.

Analogously, using (
6.5c
) we get

\displaystyle\bigl|\bigl\langle{b_{\alpha}}\otimes\mathds{1}\otimes\mathds{1}-\mathcal{V}^{\Delta,n}_{\alpha\beta,12}(b_{\beta})-\mathcal{V}^{\Delta,n}_{\alpha^{2}\beta,13}(b_{\beta})\big|\mathcal{V}^{\Delta,n}_{\alpha^{2}\beta,13}(b_{\beta})\bigr\rangle\bigr|

\displaystyle=\bigl|\bigl\langle{b_{\alpha}}\otimes\mathds{1}-V^{\Delta,n}_{\alpha^{2}\beta}(b_{\beta})\big|V^{\Delta,n}_{\alpha^{2}\beta}(b_{\beta})\bigr\rangle\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\bigl(M(1+D_{n})^{\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}}+D_{n}\bigr)\|a-b_{\varepsilon}\|_{2}.

Thus

(6.7)

\displaystyle\|b_{\alpha}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\bigl\|\bigl(b_{\alpha}\otimes\mathds{1}\otimes\mathds{1}-\mathcal{V}^{\Delta,n}_{\alpha\beta,12}(b_{\beta})-\mathcal{V}^{\Delta,n}_{\alpha^{2}\beta,13}(b_{\beta})\bigr)+\mathcal{V}^{\Delta,n}_{\alpha\beta,12}(b_{\beta})+\mathcal{V}^{\Delta,n}_{\alpha^{2}\beta,13}(b_{\beta})\bigr\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\bigl\|b_{\alpha}\otimes\mathds{1}\otimes\mathds{1}-\mathcal{V}^{\Delta,n}_{\alpha\beta,12}(b_{\beta})-\mathcal{V}^{\Delta,n}_{\alpha^{2}\beta,13}(b_{\beta})\bigr\|_{2}^{2}+2(1-D_{n})\|b_{\beta}\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle-4\bigl(M(1+D_{n})^{\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}}+D_{n}\bigr)\|a-b_{\varepsilon}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq 2(1-D_{n})\|b_{\beta}\|^{2}_{2}-4\bigl(M(1+D_{n})^{\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}}+D_{n}\bigr)\|a-b_{\varepsilon}\|_{2}.

Now we combine inequalities (
6.6
) and (
6.7
):

\displaystyle\|b_{\alpha}\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle\geq 2(1-D_{n})\Bigl((1-D_{n})\|b_{\alpha}\|_{2}^{2}-2\bigl(M(1+D_{n})^{\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}}+D_{n}\bigr)\|a-b_{\varepsilon}\|_{2}\Bigr)-4\bigl(M(1+D_{n})^{\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}}+D_{n}\bigr)\|a-b_{\varepsilon}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=2(1-D_{n})^{2}\|b_{\alpha}\|_{2}^{2}-4(2-D_{n})\bigl(M(1+D_{n})^{\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}}+D_{n}\bigr)\|a-b_{\varepsilon}\|_{2}

and as a consequence obtain

(6.8)

\|b_{\alpha}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\tfrac{4(2-D_{n})}{2(1-D_{n})^{2}-1}\bigl(M(1+D_{n})^{\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}}+D_{n}\bigr)\|a-b_{\varepsilon}\|_{2}.

Similarly

\displaystyle\|b_{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq(1-D_{n})\Bigl(2(1-D_{n})\|b_{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}-4\bigl(M(1+D_{n})^{\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}}+D_{n}\bigr)\|a-b_{\varepsilon}\|_{2}\Bigr)-2\bigl(M(1+D_{n})^{\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}}+D_{n}\bigr)\|a-b_{\varepsilon}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=2(1-D_{n})^{2}\|b_{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}-\bigl(4(1-D_{n})+2\bigr)\bigl(M(1+D_{n})^{\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}}+D_{n}\bigr)\|a-b_{\varepsilon}\|_{2}

leads to

(6.9)

\|b_{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\tfrac{4(1-D_{n})+2}{2(1-D_{n})^{2}-1}\bigl(M(1+D_{n})^{\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}}+D_{n}\bigr)\|a-b_{\varepsilon}\|_{2}.

Inequalities (
6.8
), (
6.9
) combine to

\displaystyle\|a-b_{\varepsilon}\|^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle=\|b_{\alpha}+b_{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}=\|b_{\alpha}\|_{2}^{2}+\|b_{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Bigl(\tfrac{4(2-D_{n})}{2(1-D_{n})^{2}-1}+\tfrac{4(1-D_{n})+2}{2(1-D_{n})^{2}-1}\Bigr)\bigl(M(1+D_{n})^{\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}}+D_{n}\bigr)\|a-b_{\varepsilon}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{2(7-4D_{n})}{2(1-D_{n})^{2}-1}\bigl(M(1+D_{n})^{\scriptscriptstyle{1\>\!\!/\;\!\!2}}+D_{n}\bigr)\|a-b_{\varepsilon}\|_{2}

which ends the proof.
∎

As a corollary we obtain

Theorem 6.7
.

Take
n\in\mathbb{N}
and assume that the constant
D_{n}
defined by (
6.3
) satisfies
D_{n}<1-\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}
and
\tfrac{2(7-4D_{n})D_{n}}{2(1-D_{n})^{2}-1}<\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{n+1}}
. Then
\widehat{\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}}
is
n
-i.c.c., i.e.
\Delta^{(n)}(\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}))^{\prime}\cap\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\overset{\rule[-1.0pt]{5.0pt}{0.5pt}}{\otimes}(n+1)}=\mathbb{C}\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}
.

The conditions
D_{n}<1-\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}
,
\tfrac{2(7-4D_{n})D_{n}}{2(1-D_{n})^{2}-1}<\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{n+1}}
can be translated to conditions on matrix
F
. Furthermore,
D_{n}=D_{\alpha^{2}\beta,n}
, see Corollary
6.9
.

Proof of Theorem
6.7
.

Take a non-zero
c\in\Delta^{(n)}(\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}))^{\prime}\cap\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\overset{\rule[-1.0pt]{5.0pt}{0.5pt}}{\otimes}(n+1)}
and assume that
c\not\in\mathbb{C}\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}
. Setting

a=\frac{c-{\boldsymbol{h}}^{\otimes(n+1)}(c)\,\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}}{\|c-{\boldsymbol{h}}^{\otimes(n+1)}(c)\,\mathds{1}^{\otimes(n+1)}\|_{2}}

we obtain
a\in\Delta^{(n)}(\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}))^{\prime}\cap\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+})^{\overset{\rule[-1.0pt]{5.0pt}{0.5pt}}{\otimes}(n+1)}
,
\|a\|_{2}=1
and
{\boldsymbol{h}}^{\otimes(n+1)}(a)=0
. Write as in the proof of Proposition
6.6

(6.10)

a=\sum_{x_{1},\dotsc,x_{n+1}\in\{\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta\}}a_{x_{1},\dotsc,x_{n+1}}.

and

b_{k,x}=\sum_{x_{i}\in\{\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta\},\>i\neq{k}}a_{x_{1},\dotsc,x_{k-1},x,x_{k+1},\dotsc,x_{n+1}},\qquad{k}\in\{1,\dotsc,n+1\},\;x\in\{\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta\}.

Since
a
belongs to
\Delta^{(n)}(\Linf(\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}))^{\prime}
and
\|a\|_{2}=1
, Proposition
6.6
gives us

\|b_{k,\alpha}\|_{2}^{2}+\|b_{k,\beta}\|_{2}^{2}=\|b_{k,\alpha}+b_{k,\beta}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\bigl(\tfrac{2(7-4D_{n})}{2(1-D_{n})^{2}-1}D_{n}\bigr)^{2}

for
1\leq{k}\leq{n+1}
, hence as
1=\|a\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{x\in\{\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta\}}\|b_{k,x}\|_{2}^{2}
we obtain

\|b_{k,\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\geq{1}-\Bigl(\tfrac{2(7-4D_{n})}{2(1-D_{n})^{2}-1}D_{n}\Bigr)^{2}

and consequently

(6.11)

\sum_{k=1}^{n+1}\|b_{k,\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\geq(n+1)\biggl(1-\Bigl(\tfrac{2(7-4D_{n})}{2(1-D_{n})^{2}-1}D_{n}\Bigr)^{2}\biggr).

On the other hand, since the decomposition in (
6.10
) is orthogonal

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n+1}\|b_{k,\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{n+1}\biggl\|\sum_{x_{i}\in\{\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta\},\>i\neq{k}}a_{x_{1},\dotsc,x_{k-1},\varepsilon,x_{k+1},\dotsc,x_{n+1}}\biggr\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{n+1}\sum_{x_{i}\in\{\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta\},\>i\neq{k}}\bigl\|a_{x_{1},\dotsc,x_{k-1},\varepsilon,x_{k+1},\dotsc,x_{n+1}}\bigr\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq n\sum_{x_{1},\dotsc,x_{n+1}\in\{\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta\}}\|a_{x_{1},\dotsc,x_{n+1}}\|_{2}^{2}=n.

The reason why in the above inequality we have number
n
, and not
n+1
is that every term
\|a_{x_{1},\dotsc,x_{n+1}}\|_{2}^{2}
appears at most
n
times, unless
(x_{1},\dotsc,x_{n+1})=(\varepsilon,\dotsc,\varepsilon)
, but
a_{\varepsilon,\dotsc,\varepsilon}=0
. Together with inequality (
6.11
) and assumption
\tfrac{2(7-4D_{n})D_{n}}{2(1-D_{n})^{2}-1}<\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{n+1}}
it gives a contradiction because

(n+1)\biggl(1-\Bigl(\tfrac{2(7-4D_{n})}{2(1-D_{n})^{2}-1}D_{n}\Bigr)^{2}\biggr)\leq\sum_{k=1}^{n+1}\|b_{k,\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}\leq{n}

implies
\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{n+1}}\leq\tfrac{2(7-4D_{n})}{2(1-D_{n})^{2}-1}D_{n}
.
∎

In the last result of this section we obtain a sufficient condition under which
\widehat{\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}}
is
n
-i.c.c. We note that one can easily obtain stronger (although less transparent) conditions using (
6.12
).

Lemma 6.8
.

For
n\in\mathbb{N}
we have
D_{n}=D_{\alpha^{2}\beta,n}
.

Corollary 6.9
.

Take
n\in\mathbb{N}
and write
\mathfrak{c}=\max\bigl\{\|\lambda{F^{*}F}-\mathds{1}\|,\|(\lambda{F^{*}F})^{-1}-\mathds{1}\|\bigr\}
,
where
\lambda=\sqrt{\tfrac{\Tr((F^{*}F)^{-1})}{\Tr(F^{*}F)}}
. If

\sqrt{n}(n+1)\mathfrak{c}(2+\mathfrak{c})(1+\mathfrak{c})^{4+6n}<\tfrac{1}{72},

then
\widehat{\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}}
is
n
-i.c.c. In particular
\widehat{\operatorname{U}_{N}^{+}}
is
n
-i.c.c. for any
N\geq{2}
.

Proof of Lemma
6.8
and Corollary
6.9
.

Recall that by definition

D_{n}=\max\bigl\{D_{\alpha\beta,n},D_{\beta\alpha,n},D_{\alpha^{2}\beta,n}\bigr\},

where

D_{x,n}=\begin{cases}\|\uprho_{x}^{2}-\mathds{1}\|\tfrac{\|\uprho_{x}\|^{2(n+1)}-1}{\|\uprho_{x}\|^{2}-1}&\uprho_{x}\neq\mathds{1}\\
0&\uprho_{x}=\mathds{1}\end{cases},\qquad{x}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}\star\mathbb{Z}_{+}.

The fusion rules of
\operatorname{U}_{F}^{+}
(equation (
3.6
)) show
\alpha\text{\tiny$\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.40282pt\hskip-3.40282pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\bigcirc$}$}\hskip-3.40282pt\hskip-2.70836pt\raisebox{-0.85208pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\top$}$}}\hskip-2.70836pt\hskip 3.40282pt}}$}\beta=\alpha\beta\oplus\varepsilon
and
\beta\text{\tiny$\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.40282pt\hskip-3.40282pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\bigcirc$}$}\hskip-3.40282pt\hskip-2.70836pt\raisebox{-0.85208pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\top$}$}}\hskip-2.70836pt\hskip 3.40282pt}}$}\alpha=\beta\alpha\oplus\varepsilon
, hence using
[
28
, Theorem 1.4.9]
we obtain

\|\uprho_{\alpha\beta}\|=\|\uprho_{\beta\alpha}\|,\quad\|\uprho_{\alpha\beta}^{2}-\mathds{1}\|=\|\uprho_{\beta\alpha}^{2}-\mathds{1}\|.

Next,
\alpha\text{\tiny$\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.40282pt\hskip-3.40282pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\bigcirc$}$}\hskip-3.40282pt\hskip-2.70836pt\raisebox{-0.85208pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\top$}$}}\hskip-2.70836pt\hskip 3.40282pt}}$}\alpha\beta=\alpha^{2}\beta
, hence writing
\simeq
for unitary equivalence, we have
\uprho_{\alpha}\otimes\uprho_{\alpha\beta}\simeq\uprho_{\alpha^{2}\beta}
and

\|\uprho_{\alpha^{2}\beta}\|=\|\uprho_{\alpha}\|\|\uprho_{\alpha\beta}\|\geq\|\uprho_{\alpha\beta}\|

and

\|\uprho_{\alpha^{2}\beta}^{2}-\mathds{1}\|=\max\bigl\{|\mu|\,\bigr|\bigl.\,\mu\in\operatorname{Sp}(\uprho_{\alpha^{2}\beta}^{2}-\mathds{1})\bigr\}=\max\bigl\{|\mu-1|\,\bigr|\bigl.\,\mu\in\operatorname{Sp}(\uprho_{\alpha}^{2}\otimes\uprho_{\alpha\beta}^{2})\bigr\}\geq\|\uprho_{\alpha\beta}^{2}-\mathds{1}\|

(note that
\max\operatorname{Sp}(\uprho_{\alpha})\geq{1},\min\operatorname{Sp}(\uprho_{\alpha})\leq{1}
). Consequently

D_{n}=D_{\alpha^{2}\beta,n}.

To use Theorem
6.7
, we need to check
D_{n}<1-\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}
and
\tfrac{2(7-4D_{n})D_{n}}{2(1-D_{n})^{2}-1}<\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{n+1}}
. Clearly these conditions are met if
\uprho_{\alpha}=\mathds{1}
(as then
D_{n}=0
), hence from now on assume otherwise.

As mentioned after Proposition
5.1
, by
[
28
, Examples 1.4.2]
we have
\uprho_{\alpha}=\lambda(F^{*}F)^{\top}
, hence by
[
28
, Proposition 1.4.7]
,
\uprho_{\beta}=\lambda^{-1}(F^{*}F)^{-1}
. Since
\alpha\text{\tiny$\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.40282pt\hskip-3.40282pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\bigcirc$}$}\hskip-3.40282pt\hskip-2.70836pt\raisebox{-0.85208pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\top$}$}}\hskip-2.70836pt\hskip 3.40282pt}}$}\alpha\text{\tiny$\mathbin{\hbox{\hskip 3.40282pt\hskip-3.40282pt\hbox{$\hbox{$\bigcirc$}$}\hskip-3.40282pt\hskip-2.70836pt\raisebox{-0.85208pt}{\hbox{$\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\rule{0.0pt}{0.0pt}\hbox{$\top$}$}}\hskip-2.70836pt\hskip 3.40282pt}}$}\beta=\alpha^{2}\beta\oplus\alpha
, we have
\uprho_{\alpha}\otimes\uprho_{\alpha}\otimes\uprho_{\beta}\simeq\uprho_{\alpha^{2}\beta}\oplus\uprho_{\alpha}
and

(6.12)

\|\uprho_{\alpha^{2}\beta}\|=\lambda\|F^{*}F\|^{2}\|(F^{*}F)^{-1}\|,\quad\|\uprho_{\alpha^{2}\beta}^{2}-\mathds{1}\|=\|\lambda^{2}(F^{*}F)^{2}\otimes(F^{*}F)^{2}\otimes(F^{*}F)^{-2}-\mathds{1}\otimes\mathds{1}\otimes\mathds{1}\|.

Writing
\mathfrak{c}=\max\bigl\{\|\lambda{F^{*}F}-\mathds{1}\|,\bigl\|(\lambda{F^{*}F})^{-1}-\mathds{1}\bigr\|\bigr\}
we have
\max\bigl\{\|\lambda{F^{*}F}\|,\bigl\|(\lambda{F^{*}F})^{-1}\bigr\|\bigr\}\leq{1}+\mathfrak{c}
and
\|\uprho_{\alpha^{2}\beta}\|\leq(1+\mathfrak{c})^{3}
. Next

\displaystyle\bigl\|\uprho_{\alpha^{2}\beta}^{2}-\mathds{1}\bigr\|

\displaystyle=\bigl\|(\lambda{F^{*}F})^{2}-\mathds{1}\bigr\|\bigl\|(F^{*}F)^{2}\bigr\|\bigl\|(F^{*}F)^{-2}\bigr\|+\|(F^{*}F)^{2}\otimes(F^{*}F)^{-2}-\mathds{1}\otimes\mathds{1}\|

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|(\lambda{F^{*}F})^{2}-\mathds{1}\bigr\|\|F^{*}F\|^{2}\bigl\|(F^{*}F)^{-1}\bigr\|^{2}+\bigl\|(\lambda{F^{*}F})^{2}-\mathds{1}\bigr\|\bigl\|(\lambda{F^{*}F})^{-1}\bigr\|^{2}+\bigl\|(\lambda{F^{*}F})^{-2}-\mathds{1}\bigr\|

\displaystyle=\bigl\|(\lambda{F^{*}F})^{2}-\mathds{1}\bigr\|\bigl\|(\lambda{F^{*}F})^{-1}\bigr\|^{2}\bigl(\bigl\|\lambda{F^{*}F}\bigr\|^{2}+1\bigr)+\bigl\|(\lambda{F^{*}F})^{-2}-\mathds{1}\bigr\|.

Hence, since
\bigl\|(\lambda{F^{*}F})^{2}-\mathds{1}\bigr\|=\bigl\|(\lambda{F^{*}F}+\mathds{1})(\lambda{F^{*}F}-\mathds{1})\bigr\|\leq(2+\mathfrak{c})\mathfrak{c}
(and similarly
\bigl\|(\lambda{F^{*}F})^{-2}-\mathds{1}\bigr\|\leq(2+\mathfrak{c})\mathfrak{c}
), we have

\|\uprho_{\alpha^{2}\beta}^{2}-\mathds{1}\|\leq(2+\mathfrak{c})\mathfrak{c}(1+\mathfrak{c})^{2}\bigl((1+\mathfrak{c})^{2}+1\bigr)+(2+\mathfrak{c})\mathfrak{c}=\mathfrak{c}(2+\mathfrak{c})\bigl((1+\mathfrak{c})^{4}+(1+\mathfrak{c})^{2}+1\bigr)\leq 3\mathfrak{c}(2+\mathfrak{c})(1+\mathfrak{c})^{4}.

It follows that

\displaystyle D_{n}

\displaystyle=\|\uprho_{\alpha^{2}\beta}^{2}-\mathds{1}\|\frac{\|\uprho_{\alpha^{2}\beta}\|^{2(n+1)}-1}{\|\uprho_{\alpha^{2}\beta}\|^{2}-1}\leq 3\mathfrak{c}(2+\mathfrak{c})(1+\mathfrak{c})^{4}\tfrac{(1+\mathfrak{c})^{6(n+1)}-1}{(1+\mathfrak{c})^{6}-1}

\displaystyle=3\mathfrak{c}(2+\mathfrak{c})(1+\mathfrak{c})^{4}\sum_{k=0}^{n}(1+\mathfrak{c})^{6k}\leq 3(n+1)\mathfrak{c}(2+\mathfrak{c})(1+\mathfrak{c})^{4+6n}<1-\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}

by our assumption. Next we need to check that
\tfrac{2(7-4D_{n})D_{n}}{2(1-D_{n})^{2}-1}<\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{n+1}}
, equivalently

2(1+4\sqrt{n+1})D_{n}^{2}-2(2+7\sqrt{n+1})D_{n}+1>0.

An elementary argument shows that this inequality is satisfied when

(6.13)

0<D_{n}<\tfrac{2+7\sqrt{n+1}-\sqrt{49{n}+20\sqrt{n+1}+51}}{2(1+4\sqrt{n+1})}.

Since

\tfrac{2+7\sqrt{n+1}-\sqrt{49n+20\sqrt{n+1}+51}}{2(1+4\sqrt{n+1})}=\tfrac{1}{7\sqrt{n}}\,\bigl(\tfrac{2}{7\sqrt{n}}+\sqrt{1+1/n}+\sqrt{1+\tfrac{20}{49\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{1+1/n}+\tfrac{51}{49n}}\bigr)^{-1}\geq\tfrac{1}{24\sqrt{n}}

and
D_{n}\leq 3(n+1)\mathfrak{c}(2+\mathfrak{c})(1+\mathfrak{c})^{4+6n}<\tfrac{1}{24\sqrt{n}}
by assumption, inequality (
6.13
) holds and the claim follows.
∎
